| 51 | **A tripwire whose CAP was quoted as its count, and a bookkeeping counter read as a fact about the guest** | Two errors from one hour on ArcRunner (#1226), plus a third caught in review. `PROSPER_FORGE_TRIP` printed exactly `if (n++ < 64)` lines, so "the tripwire fires **64 times**, every hit identical" — restated across sessions as a measurement — was the **limit**, indistinguishable from 64,000 hits; the neighbouring `PROSPER_MB3_FREELIST_GUARD` figure it was compared against ("64 suppressed live-protocol writes per run") may or may not be the same population, and neither log could say which: that one prints sparsely past 64 but carries no ordinal, so its 64 lines bound its population only at at most 4,096 (the 65th line lands on the 4,097th event). With an ordinal added the real population exceeds **1,024**. **A capped diagnostic must carry its ordinal and keep printing sparsely past the cap**, or its ceiling becomes the finding — and the cap must be **per value/per key**, or a noisy control starves the log of the thing under investigation. The second error: the guard beside it suppressed *none* of those writes, and the reason was `live_pair` = `dma_exec_n > rel_exec_n`, read as "the guest still owns this label, so the write is correct, so the tripwire is a false positive". Those counters are **prosper's own record of which of its writes it has performed**. They are *necessary* for the live-fence story and nowhere near *sufficient* — `waf_guard()`'s comment already documented the case that produces them on an **already-freed** block — and the conclusion was one edit away from a doc row declaring the lead dead. What settled it was making the two hypotheses predict **different observables** instead of arguing about the same ones: the forged value is a pointer with its low bit set, so print what a misaligned dereference of it would return. It returned `0x30016000` on every *reported* hit — byte-for-byte the constant the terminal fault dereferences — and the disassembled pop showed one pop both returning that node (the guest's own `unrecognized block 2000000001` fatal) and storing the misread as the next head (the SIGSEGV). That collapsed two "separate legs" tracked since #1249 into one chain. **Third error, caught only in review: the replacement instrument written to document all this shipped with a bare 256-line cap, no ordinal, a budget shared across armed values, and a doc paragraph claiming the total was visible — and its author then wrote "256 hits" in the same comment.** Knowing the rule is not protection; the defence is mechanical (make the cap per key, print the ordinal, and re-read your own numbers as if someone else had produced them). Cousin of #46 and #34. |
| 52 | **A counter-arm that REMOVES a feature instead of BREAKING it, so the tests SKIP rather than fail** | Verifying #1757, the author's mutation reverted the **registration** of the new HLE function and reported the suite turning red as proof the new tests were live. It proved nothing. The tests begin `auto waitvbl = Hle::lookup(...); CHECK(waitvbl != nullptr, ...); if (waitvbl) { ...three behavioural checks... }` — with the registration gone the lookup returns null, **the guarded block never executes**, and the single red line is the registration check reporting on itself. The three assertions that matter were never run in either arm. Reviewed independently, the real arms are: **A** registration kept, body reverted to `return 0` -> 3 named failures; **B** registration kept, body kept, the shared epoch replaced by a private one shifted half a period -> **exactly 1 failure**, which is the discriminating run because it shows *which* assertion carries the claim. **The rule: revert the BEHAVIOUR, never the registration, the wiring, the build entry or the test's own precondition** — otherwise you are testing whether the test runs, not whether it works. This is the escalation of #48 ("a mutation that turns the suite red, read as proof that the check you wrote for it works") and its mechanism is the one #34 names: a **skipped** check and a **passing** check are byte-identical in a green suite, so the guard pattern that makes a test suite robust — `if (looked_up) { … }`, `if (file_exists) { … }`, an `EXPECT_*` inside a conditional — is exactly what makes this mutation silent. The tell is cheap and mechanical: **count the assertions that RAN in each arm, not the ones that failed.** If the arm you expect to fail executes fewer checks than the baseline, the mutation removed the test rather than breaking the code. The same review found a live survivor in the same file by the same discipline — an assertion named "shares GetVblankStatus's timebase" that only ever tested the *period* and passed a half-period phase shift, i.e. it could not fail for the reason its name claimed. |
| 53 | **A scripted edit that CONFIRMS the new text is present, without checking it replaced the old** | Correcting two withdrawn claims in `COMPATIBILITY.md` (#1767), the edit script computed each old row as a slice `s[s.index(A):s.index(B)]` and called `s.replace(old, new, 1)`. One slice came out **empty**, and `str.replace("", new, 1)` does not fail — it **prepends**. So the corrected row landed as line 1 of a public document, *above the H1*, while the row carrying the withdrawn claim stayed exactly where it was, inside `### Ruled out`. The author verified with `grep -c` for the new text and got `1`. **That check passed, and it was incapable of failing**: it asked *is the new text in the file* when the question was *did the new text replace the old*, and both a correct substitution and a prepend-plus-original answer `1`. Cousin of #34, specialised to editing: an insertion and a substitution differ only in what is ABSENT afterwards, so the verification must assert the absence. Three rules, all cheap. **Assert the old text is gone**, not just that the new text is there. **Assert the document's invariants** — the file still starts with its H1, every table row still has the same cell count as its header (that one alone catches a dropped `Ref` cell, which this same edit also did, twice) — because a structural check does not care how the damage happened. Count cells on **unescaped** pipes and nothing else — GFM splits a row into cells *before* it parses inline content, so a pipe inside a code span genuinely IS a cell boundary and has to be written `\|`. **This row used to prescribe the opposite** — exclude code-span pipes from the count, on the belief that `ptr\|member`-style spans false-positive on legitimate rows — and that is backwards: it suppresses the real defect instead of finding it. **No legitimate row of that shape existed to false-positive on, under either reading of "this very file"**: `COMPATIBILITY.md`, which this row is about, had none when the advice was written (`7593dabb`) and has none now; `GAME_COMPAT_ORCHESTRATION.md` had four, and all four were the defect rather than legitimate rows. Meanwhile six rows of the trap table below were rendering truncated on GitHub, trap 40 among them — three lost issue citations into the discarded region and one lost a dedicated citation cell outright. `prosper/tools/docs/check_numbered_table.py` now enforces cell arity in CI (#2108). And **make the script assert its own preconditions before writing**: `assert old in s`, or pin indices and assert their geometry, so an anchor that silently stops matching aborts instead of degrading into a no-op or a prepend. The failure is invisible to review-by-diff-summary too: the diff stat read `+4 −2` on the right file, which is what a correct edit looks like. Note where this one sat — not in an instrument, not in a measurement, but in the *edit that records the finding*, which is the last place anyone looks and the one that outlives the session. |
| 54 | **A substitution that restores a missing INPUT, read as making the consumer RUN** | On #1590 the rejected Earthion composite was missing one of its two texture descriptors, so a gated switch supplied it — purely to answer "if this dropped draw executed, would anything appear?" by eye. The A/B ran through the headless `screenshot` frontend and produced two visually identical frames, one edit from being recorded as *"executing this draw changes nothing on screen"*. **The draw never executed in either arm.** The substitution did exactly what it claimed — the resource table went from `(1 res)` to `(2 res)` — but the *consumer* failed for a second, independent reason: the shader copies its T# out of user data with eight `s_mov_b32`, so the SRSRC range reads as written, the direct `by_sgpr_base` lookup is skipped, and no `s_load` SRT tag exists to key on. Both arms emitted the same 14 `[exec-recompile-reject]` lines climbing to `occurrence=8192`. The tell was one grep of a log already on disk, and it is the rule: **count the events the experiment was supposed to eliminate, in BOTH arms, before looking at the output** — an A/B whose arms still fail for a shared reason renders identically *for that reason*, and a screenshot cannot say so. Note the direction relative to #52: there a counter-arm removed a feature so the checks skipped; here the experimental arm removed the symptom's first cause while a second cause held the symptom in place. Same failure of attribution, opposite end of the pipeline. The fabricating switch was never landed — output produced by forced guest state is evidence about a pass, never about correctness. |
| 55 | **A buffer-full event with space still left, read as "prosper asked for more than the guest budgeted"** | The #1756 probe's own first reading rule, falsified by the corpus it was built to survey. `AgcDcb::available_dw()` subtracts the guest's `reserved_dw`, so any title holding a reserve reaches *space left, but not enough* at the end of **every** buffer, for whatever packet arrives next. Corpus-wide every repeated such triple carries `reserved=18`, with the leftover tracking each packet's own size (`need=3 avail=2` … `need=69 avail=25`) — which read naively says prosper is oversized on seven different builders by seven different amounts. It cost a reported candidate: `SetShRegsIndirect need=4 avail=3` in three titles is the exact arithmetic of #1748's off-by-one and is an artefact. The signature needs `reserved=0` **and** a repeating triple, and it is *sufficient, not necessary* — an overrun under a live reserve is still invisible to it. |
| 56 | **A command-buffer window, read as a reservation** | Same probe, same hour. `cursor_down - bottom` is the current segment of a segmented Dcb, not a size the guest budgeted: The Pathless produces 58 buffer-full events on windows of 1–7 dwords, every one with the cursor exactly at the limit. A window is only a reservation when a *tight* one holds a known packet sequence — which is what made Babylon's 16-dword epilogue evidence (#1748) and makes these 58 noise. |
| 57 | **The interval between two late wakes, read as the current wake's lateness** | #1771 tried to make `videoout_queries` self-invalidating under Rosetta load by admitting a phase sample only when one `WaitVblank` call took less than one period plus 4 ms. Its premise was that a wait takes `period + current_lateness`. A wait starts *after the previous wake* and targets the next boundary, so consecutive duration is approximately `period - previous_lateness + current_lateness`: if the scheduler is consistently 8 ms late, the latenesses cancel, duration still reads one period, and the >4 ms phase sample is admitted and fails. Both attempts of unrelated PR #1789 reproduced exactly that on macOS x86_64/Rosetta (#1793). **An elapsed interval cannot isolate one endpoint's error when both endpoints move.** Nor can another host-timing threshold solve the identifiability problem: scheduler delay and a shifted grid produce the same late return. The platform-stable discriminator is a deterministic clock/sleeper seam underneath the real HLE calls: positive-control that the fake clock reached `GetVblankStatus`, let `WaitVblank` advance it, then assert its returned boundary belongs to the status grid. A half-period private epoch now fails exactly the named phase check while the real five-wait blocking guard remains on the host clock. |
| 58 | **A handler-entry count, read as completion of asynchronous delivery** | `win_exception_delivery`'s 100-delivery stress waited for the guest handler to increment `delivered`, then immediately raised again. On the forced Windows path that increment happens on the dedicated exception stack **before** `RtlRestoreContext`; the delivery slot intentionally remains active until a later raiser suspends the target and proves its RSP has left that stack. The next raise could therefore observe the handler count and still legitimately return EBUSY. The same exact head failed once and passed on rerun, while its R12-preservation and target-resume checks stayed green (#1798). A later two-delivery CPU-fallback check made the same inference and carried the same latent race. A sleep or EBUSY retry would hide either race without identifying completion. The causal oracle now comes from the other side of the boundary: the target's restored normal-context loop copies each observed `delivered` generation to an acknowledgment while R12 remains live, and both raisers wait for that acknowledgment before proceeding. Removing only the acknowledgment store makes both causal delivery checks fail — including the named 100-delivery check — while R12 preservation and target resumption still pass. **Entry is not completion; for asynchronous work, acknowledge from the resumed consumer context after the boundary whose completion you claim.** |
| 59 | **A timeline's address-copy census, read as a census of every `DMA_DATA` packet** | Astro Bot's retained frame-17 timelines reported `dmas=0`, which was one edit away from becoming evidence that no GDS counter reset preceded the runaway dispatch (#1732). Through timeline v9, `dmas` came exclusively from `GpuState::dma_copies`: the ordered-execution vector for **address-backed copies**. Immediate fills, GDS-offset destinations, and packets later rejected by execution never entered that vector, so a submit containing a decoded GDS reset and a submit containing no `DMA_DATA` at all produced the same zero. The old captures therefore cannot answer reset-versus-dispatch ordering; their raw journal is **unavailable**, not empty. Timeline v10 makes the populations explicit: `dma-copies` remains the address-backed execution record, while `dmas` is the raw journal's original 64-bit count, taken before execution classification. The retained raw records are bounded at 8,192 and carry exact operands, selectors, PM4 order and packet address; an explicit truncation flag distinguishes `count > retained` from a complete journal, and the format rejects inconsistent count/truncation combinations. Collection is enabled only when the run starts with `PROSPER_GPU_TIMELINE`; without it the decode path performs no raw-journal count, allocation, or record writes, and the missing records cannot be recovered later. Thus a non-truncated v10 zero **from an enabled journal** proves only that no packet was decoded — a raw entry is an observation, never proof that execution succeeded. **Before reading a diagnostic count, identify its producer, population, enablement and format version; an unavailable population must never be converted to zero.** |
| 60 | **A title boot without `PROSPER_RENDER`, read as GPU-free** | Asterix's semantic-timeline run deliberately omitted live graphics and was scheduled as a CPU-only experiment. The premise was false: `boot_trace` still registered the live compute backend, and the first supported dispatch initialized the Vulkan device and executed the compute seed-skip verification. `PROSPER_RENDER` gates graphics rendering; it does not turn a title boot into a decoder-only run. This matters operationally on the shared workstation: an apparently headless timeline can contend with another lane's performance measurement while its own log still looks like a cheap semantic index. **Treat every title boot as GPU work unless the exact runner configuration independently proves that no graphics or compute backend can initialize.** Offline `gpu_timeline`, `gpu_replay --inspect-only`/`--graph`, bundle extraction, and standalone `shader_inspect` remain CPU-only; other `gpu_replay` dump options require the control-flow classification in #63. Rendering a replay is GPU work. |
| 61 | **A shader-draw dependency model, applied to a fixed-function resolve** | Asterix's retained 14-operation submit first produced `edges=0 external-leaves=15`; specifically, the graph called pixel-shader binding 34 at `0x2011800000` an external leaf consumed by operations 8/11. Operations 5/6/9/10 were `CB_COLOR_CONTROL.MODE=RESOLVE`, for which the live backend executes no shader: it copies raw color0 to color1 despite color1's zero shader write mask. The graph instead invented color0 writes, omitted color1 writes, and counted resources from shaders that never ran. Resolve-aware graphing produces the exact chain 4→5, 4→6, 6→8, 4→9, 4→10, 10→11 and no external image leaf for that composite source. A source format is also part of the dependency: an external FP16 resolve source must remain FP16 or an exact temporal seed cannot close it safely. **Classify an operation by the backend mechanism that actually executes before interpreting its shader resources or export masks; fixed-function work needs explicit read/write semantics.** |
| 62 | **A capture with complete payload bytes, read as a self-contained record of the live resource** | R-Type's retained movie frame became detailed after its texel-coordinate fix but stayed green/purple, apparently localising a second live renderer defect. The payload looked conclusive: complete R8 Y and RG8 UV planes, exact T# swizzle `(X,Y,0,1)`, and a coherent BT.709 shader. It was the apparatus. F9 retained the plane bytes but recorded both resources with `linear_row_pitch_bytes=0`; replay also lacks the live process's AvPlayer pitch registry. Without either provenance arm, `avplayer_chroma_layout` intentionally falls back to the historical narrow-coverage path and broadcasts the first byte, turning `(U,V)` into `(U,U)`. The discriminator is unusually strong: 494,043/552,960 captured U/V pairs differ, yet an independent BT.709 `(U,U)` model matches replay at 42.17 dB / 0.9816 SSIM while correct `(U,V)` does not. The screenshot therefore proves coordinate/detail recovery and says nothing about live chroma. This is distinct from #59's unavailable population converted to zero: here **the payload is present and correct, but missing interpretation provenance selects a different transform**. A capture is self-contained only if it retains every side table, registry fact, layout field, feature/policy bit and temporal dependency that can change how those bytes are consumed—or explicitly marks the missing fact unavailable. Before reading replay output as live evidence, enumerate the live branch predicates and prove replay took the same branch; otherwise validate the apparatus first, not the title. |
| 63 | **A `gpu_replay` dump option, read as extraction-only** | During Asterix's retained-capture follow-up, `--dump-realized-shader` and `--dump-resource` were scheduled as CPU inspection because both write a requested artifact. On current master each did write its artifact, then **fell through to renderer registration and a full Vulkan replay**. The two unintended runs were visible only because the log continued with the Vulkan device and final output hash; both completed before the mistake was noticed. In the same observed path, `--dump-shader` returned immediately and did not initialize Vulkan. The rule is narrower and mechanical: **an option that dumps a CPU-readable artifact is not thereby CPU-only; classify it by the control-flow return before renderer registration, and treat it as GPU work until that return is proven.** This is the command-line twin of #60: the option name describes the requested output, not every backend the command will exercise while producing it. |
| 64 | **A successfully armed per-thread breakpoint, read as coverage of every guest-execution boundary** | `PROSPER_HWBP_ALLTHREADS=1` armed `run_entry` and workers created through the HLE pthread trampoline, but `boot_program` executes dependent-module init functions **before** `run_entry`. Those functions are guest code and can themselves create guest workers; the primary perf event did not yet exist while they ran. Frontends add a second ownership wrinkle: prosper-app and screenshot initialize on their caller, then enter the main guest on a distinct `std::thread`, so treating one global fd as “the primary thread” loses the real topology. A process-thread census does not close either gap: a late host thread named after the executable is not proof that it executes guest code, while an unlisted module-init boundary can execute guest code on an already-known TID. The fix attaches arming to one explicit guest-execution boundary used by module init, `run_entry`, and HLE worker trampolines; each TID retains its own fd/step state and the first valid fd remains only an always-live signal-handler anchor. A CPU-only mutation check keeps init and frontend threads simultaneously live and proves both cross the boundary before their guest markers; deleting only the init registration leaves its guest function running and fails exactly that named check. **Instrument coverage must be stated in terms of the semantic boundary that makes an event observable, not thread creation, names, or the set of arm messages that happened to print.** #1832. |
| 65 | **A static-destructor summary, read as available from a booted `prosper-app`** | #1839 made a compute-timing run self-validating through a final `seen`/`matched` selector verdict, initially emitted only by a function-local static destructor. A booted app cannot reach that destructor: while its guest thread remains alive, the established safe teardown is `std::_Exit`, which skips all C++ static destruction. The accepted banner and first match could therefore appear while the promised zero/nonzero-match summary was structurally impossible. #1840 makes the report idempotent, calls it explicitly immediately before the app's deliberate `_Exit`, and retains the destructor as the ordinary-frontend fallback. A pure policy mutation proves the two paths cannot duplicate the verdict. **Any diagnostic whose completeness is deferred to destruction must be checked against every frontend's actual exit primitive; `_Exit` requires an explicit flush/report path.** |
| 66 | **A process-wide file-size limit, read as a cap on one redirected log — and `$!` read as the process group it launched** | An Astro exact-hash timing arm set `ulimit -f 8192` intending to keep only `run.log` below 8 MiB. `RLIMIT_FSIZE` is inherited by the subject and applies to every file it writes, not to the shell redirection: an ordinary non-log app write raised SIGXFSZ before guest boot while the log was only 806 bytes. The selector's accepted banner survived, but there was no first match or terminal summary, so the arm was **VOID**, not a title result. The corrected external watcher exposed a second ownership error: under this launcher `setsid command &` forked, so `$!` named the short-lived `setsid` parent rather than the long-lived timeout/app session; the wrapper exited before attaching and left the subject alive. | 67 | **A per-binding provenance flag, read after the binding was folded into an exact alias** | Syberia's first compute-authority census reported binding 4 of one image range as `ProvenGpuImage` and binding 5 of the same range as `GuestImage`, apparently proving that deferred authority had to return to guest memory. Binding 5 was an exact sampled-descriptor alias of binding 4. Alias folding copies the owner's concrete Vulkan image, view and sampler, then returns before repeating cache/transfer setup; the alias shell therefore retains false local borrow/persistent flags even though its descriptor binds the owner's GPU-proven source. The census read those shell-local defaults and manufactured an architectural boundary. Retained gate evidence makes the discriminator exact: binding 4 records the sole transfer hit, while bindings 5–7 have reflection records but no independent sampled-gate evaluation. **After aliasing, classify provenance from the concrete bound owner and prove image/view identity; per-binding setup flags describe how that shell was constructed, not necessarily the source it consumes.** #1854. |
| 68 | **A page-protection writer trace, read as process-wide after its first single-step window** | `mprotect(PROT_READ)` covers every thread and every known alias until the first write fault, so that first fault really is the first CPU writer to the protected page. To retain post-store bytes, however, the diagnostic must restore RW, set TF, execute one instruction, then re-protect. Another thread can write during that RW interval without faulting; even if no queued sibling fault is observed, absence is not proof that the window was clean. The dmem writer trace therefore marks event 1 `coverage-valid-before=yes`, increments `coverage-gaps` after every step, and never turns a later absence into a process-wide negative. A same-page write outside the selected byte range is especially important: it consumes the only initially complete interval even though it is not the selected writer. **The first protected-page fault has process-wide provenance; after opening the page, later events are useful history but completeness is unprovable unless a stronger stop-the-world mechanism independently closes the window.** #1902. |
| 69 | **A caller-chain plus allocation-size family, read as one unique allocation** | Asterix's four-field dmem writer arm matched a real chain-1 16 MiB allocation, armed it, and only then encountered a second exact family member. The exact GPU positive control later proved the desired resource belonged to occurrence 2, while the run contained 23 such allocations. Treating the first match as the subject would have watched a plausible but wrong address. Four-field selection therefore stays strict and invalidates on occurrence 2; the optional five-field form requests one run-relative occurrence explicitly and reports requested, observed, and selected counts. **A dynamic family identity is not an object identity. Either prove uniqueness, or select a bounded occurrence and independently prove that selected mapping equals the later subject address; never repair ambiguity with a prior run's VA.** #1902. |
| 70 | **Two page-protection owners represented by one owner's boolean** | The production dirty watch and the dmem provenance trace can cover the same physical page. Releasing a production registration used to restore RW unconditionally, leaving the trace reporting `Armed`, zero gaps, and a page that `/proc/self/maps` proved writable; production re-arm during the trace's one-instruction RW step made the inverse error and restored RO before the pending store ran. **Logical ownership and actual protection are different state. Compute protection from the union of every owner, with an explicit RW override for a pending single-step, and refuse a new/re-armed owner that cannot truthfully establish its contract during that override.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 71 | **Invalidating a diagnostic read as permission to abandon its pending TF trap** | A sibling mapping/host/GPU/physical notification can invalidate the trace after the SIGSEGV handler publishes `SingleStep` but before that thread receives `TRAP_TRACE`. Clearing the pending TID at invalidation does not clear the thread's hardware TF bit; completion then returns `NotHandled`, falls into unrelated breakpoint machinery, and may terminate without the promised invalid verdict. **Invalidity ends evidentiary trust, not signal ownership. Retain the exact pending TID until it consumes the trap, clear TF there, return an explicit invalid completion, preserve the first invalid reason, and never re-arm stale topology.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 72 | **CR2/`si_addr` read as the complete span of a memory write — then a process-wide post delta read as attribution** | A qword store beginning four bytes before the selected interval faults at that earlier address while modifying four selected bytes; start-address classification alone recorded `selected=no`. The bounded signal-safe decoder now proves contiguous spans for common MOV stores and tests interval overlap. The first correction still promoted an unrecognized off-range instruction when selected bytes changed after its step. That is also unsound: every alias is process-wide RW during the window, so a sibling can produce the delta. A deterministic real-fault canary pauses an unknown off-range store, changes a selected byte from a sibling, and requires `selected=unknown changed-during-window=yes`. **A fault address is one byte, not an operand extent, and a process-wide delta does not identify its writer. Attribute only a proved overlapping span (or a fault byte already inside the selection); otherwise retain the delta and say unknown.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 73 | **A retained historical event, read as a verdict after its selector identity invalidates** | Strict-unique dmem selection can arm occurrence 1 and observe a selected write before occurrence 2 proves that caller-chain/size family ambiguous. The event is real history about occurrence 1, but the original terminal rule checked `selected_faults` before `status=invalid reason=ambiguous-allocation` and still printed `result=writer-observed` for a subject it never identified. A real-fault canary now produces exactly that order and requires the terminal decision to become `undetermined` while retaining the event. **Evidence can survive invalidation without its attribution surviving. Derive the verdict from both the observation and a still-valid identity; never let an earlier event override a later self-invalidating selector.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 74 | **A normal-context reset seam, read as permission to replace signal-owned state** | `guest_dmem_write_trace_configure()` used to clear the pending TID and overwrite the trace even while a faulting thread owned TF between SIGSEGV and `TRAP_TRACE`. Completion then returned `NotHandled`, abandoning the same live hardware state trap 71 protects against notification-driven invalidation. The seam now refuses reconfiguration without mutation while a step is pending; a paused real-fault canary proves the original TID still completes and re-arms. **A setup/reset API is not quiescent merely because callers normally use it before a run. Enforce the ownership precondition in state, especially when reset would erase a signal obligation.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 75 | **A value-initialized post buffer, printed as observed zero bytes after the read was skipped** | `CompleteInvalid` correctly avoided dereferencing topology a sibling had removed, leaving `event.after` at C++'s zero initialization. The formatter nevertheless printed `post=0000…`, manufacturing exactly the data readers use to identify a writer. Events now carry explicit post-image availability and render the skipped read as `post=unavailable`; the real invalidation canary asserts that exact representation. **Storage being initialized says nothing about whether an observation happened. Carry availability beside retained data and make the formatter consume it, or safe defaults become fabricated evidence.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 76 | **A page-fault overlay, read as owner of TF that was already set** | HWBP, BP, and STEPWIN disable/rewrite state, set TF, and rely on the next instruction's `TRAP_TRACE` to restore it. If that instruction writes a dmem-traced page, the overlay used to claim the SIGSEGV, then run first on the completion trap, clear TF, and return—stranding the original owner with its breakpoint disabled or byte unrestored. The overlay now detects pre-owned TF in the fault context, invalidates and opens its page without claiming a step, and resumes with TF untouched. A real `int3` → protected store sequence proves the original owner receives exactly one completion. **Trap ownership is compositional state, not an implication of which handler ran last. Never set or clear TF without proving it was previously free or belongs to you.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 77 | **A compatibility wrapper's `handled` boolean, read as permission to create signal state its caller cannot service** | The retained `guest_write_watch_handle_fault(addr)` API can only tell a caller to resume; it carries neither RIP/TID nor a `SingleStep` action. Delegating it to the provenance fault path nevertheless opened every trace page RW, stored `stepping_tid=0`, and published 0—the no-owner sentinel—while nobody set TF or routed a completion. The store completed and the diagnostic remained permanently `Stepping`, RW, and non-reconfigurable. A real SIGSEGV handler using only this bool API now proves the overlapping production watch becomes Dirty and the store completes, while the diagnostic fail-visibly becomes `single-step-unavailable` with no pending event. **A legacy return type is a capability boundary. Do not create ownership obligations that its caller has no vocabulary to receive or discharge.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 78 | **A decoded operand width, added to CR2 as though the fault byte were always the operand start** | For a qword beginning at `P-4` with writable page `P-0x1000` and protected page P, CR2 reports P—the first inaccessible byte. If the selection begins at `P+4`, the real store ends at `P+3` and is disjoint, but inventing `[P,P+8)` falsely reports a selected writer. A real split-page canary now executes exactly that store and requires `selected=unknown`, unchanged selected bytes, and no writer verdict. Same-page decoded crossings remain exact; a selected CR2 byte is also exact. **Instruction width proves size, not effective address. At a page-boundary fault, either derive EA from saved operands or conservatively withhold attribution.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 79 | **A precomputed alias list, read as proof that every operation over it is allocation-free** | Three dmem SIGSEGV exits walked already-built trace pages but invalidated through the normal-context protection helper. That helper created a rollback `std::vector` and `push_back`ed once per alias, so bool-capability, pre-owned-TF, and target-copy-failure faults could enter the allocator asynchronously despite the signal path's no-allocation contract. Signal invalidation now walks the immutable page/alias vectors in place, continues opening siblings after a protection failure, preserves the first invalid reason, records partial opening as an extra coverage gap, and resumes only if either trace or production handling restored the faulting alias. A hook on the allocating helper stays at zero through both real signal branches; routing the common invalidator through that helper makes exactly those named checks fail. **Preallocation of inputs does not make an algorithm allocation-free. Audit every helper reachable from the signal path, including rollback/error handling, and test the forbidden edge rather than inferring it from successful faults.** #1902 / PR #1910. |
| 80 | A `gpu_replay` **bundle extraction option, read as an inspect-only operation** | `gpu_replay --bundle-final-capsule` sounds like file extraction, but it initializes Vulkan and executes the captured bundle before exporting the final seeded capsule. During #1903 that assumption caused an accidental roughly one-second GPU overlap with another lane; the process exited normally and the overlap was disclosed, but the other lane's concurrent sample had to be repeated cleanly. **Treat every bundle mode as GPU work unless its documented path has been proved inspect-only. Acquire the shared GPU lease before `--bundle-final-capsule`, replay, compact/extract variants of unknown execution behavior, or any other bundle operation that may construct final state; only a documented `--inspect-only` path is exempt.** #1903. |
| 81 | A rebuilt **static library or sibling frontend, read as proof that the executable being measured contains the change** | #1903 rebuilt `prosper_core`, `test_eop_write`, and `prosper-app`, then ran the separate statically linked `screenshot` frontend. CMake had no reason to relink that sibling target, so the supposedly fixed arm reproduced the old DMA rejection exactly: zero positive-control lines and 24 old-path failures. The run was void, not negative. Rebuilding the exact `screenshot` target changed its mtime/hash and `strings` then proved the gated positive-control text was present before the replacement run. **Before a live A/B, build the exact executable you will launch and prove that binary contains the lever—target name, mtime/hash, build revision, or a unique diagnostic string. Rebuilding a shared archive or another frontend does not update an already-linked static executable.** #1903. |
| 82 | A **first post-realization draw-resource census, read as proof that no later draw consumes a retained result** | Syberia's first draw after its save-warning atlas reported 700 exact resource ranges across 140 epochs, all unrelated, but that probe deliberately closed after draw one. A full-submit probe retained the same atlas identity; all 140 valid epochs had at least one later overlap and the summary counted 700 realized overlaps. Its 21 bounded detail samples consistently showed a full-size texture range at draw ordinal 34 and bindings among 35/38/39/40/41, but do not prove that population-wide distribution or the earliest overlap. Dropping the retained range after draw one makes the exact later-draw canary fail. **A zero-overlap first draw proves only that draw is unrelated. Retain identity to the real lifetime boundary, keep draw ordinals, distinguish aggregate counters from sparse details, and make early retirement a defect-shaped mutation before authorizing full-submit deferral.** #1737. |
| 83 | `prosper-app --help` or `--version`, assumed to be a **metadata-only frontend mode** | `prosper-app` does not currently consume either option as metadata. Both invocations entered the no-game UI and initialized Vulkan; two agents independently had to notice and terminate the unintended GPU process. The `--help` arm presented zero frames in about one second, while the later `--version` arm remained alive for roughly 21 seconds. Both were disclosed and followed by an exact clean process census. **Do not execute a frontend to prove its revision unless that exact option is documented as non-rendering. Inspect the binary non-executingly (`strings`, `readelf`, hash), or use source/build metadata. Treat unknown frontend options as a possible GPU run requiring a lease.** #1737. |
| 84 | A simultaneous **`PROSPER_HWBP` + `PROSPER_BP` run, read as two independent breakpoint records** | Oregon's owner arm produced one genuine HWBP at `0x901edd`, then the int3 at `0x88ae5a` arrived while an HWBP fd was live. The Linux handler's HWBP branch accepted **any** `SIGTRAP` before the BP branch could test ownership, printed the int3 event as a plausible second `[hwbp]` at `0x88ae5b`, skipped BP's restore/back-up/single-step sequence, and the subject immediately aborted with allocator corruption. The first genuine HWBP record remains usable because it preceded the int3 and independently carried `caller_rbp=eboot+0x88ae5a`; the fabricated second HWBP record and every later outcome are void. **Two instruments sharing a signal are not independent merely because both armed. Route by explicit owner state (`g_bp_stepping` / BP RIP, HWBP perf fd / step state) before mutating either state machine, and require a coexistence test that proves both original instructions execute.** **Fixed** — the Linux handler now arbitrates every `SIGTRAP` through `src/host/fault/trap_arbitration.hpp` and yields to `PROSPER_BP` whenever BP's own state proves ownership (`RIP == g_bp_addr + 1`, or `g_bp_stepping` with TF set and this TID not mid-HWBP-step), so the two instruments may be combined in one run again; `tests/host/fault/test_trap_arbitration.cpp` arms both live and reproduces the misroute when the pre-fix condition is restored. The two records above stay void — they predate the fix. The same steal of the `PROSPER_WATCH_LABEL` / `PROSPER_WATCH_COMPANION` step completions is still open. #1932, #2559. |
| 85 | An **asset load trace, read as proof that the guest executed the code that uses those assets** | Oregon Trail's `tools/re/pak_index.py --distinct` trace showed `WBP_Splash_Gameloft`, `BP_EULA_Controller`, `WBP_System_Disclaimer_Popup` and the splash logo atlas loading, which reads exactly like the shipped Blueprint's `Create` + `AddToViewport` step having run. It had not: a **pre-fix control arm loaded the identical set at the identical trace ordinals over a run 12x longer**, while demonstrably still stuck at the account gate. UE4 pulls a level's referenced classes through the map package's import table, before any Blueprint bytecode executes, so the load trace answers *what is resident*, never *what ran*. The discriminator that did work was second-order and much narrower: `.ufont` + `icudt64l/brkitr/line.brk`, which Slate touches only when it lays text out, appeared in the fixed arm alone. **Classify each asset as `referenced-by-a-loaded-package` or `loaded-only-on-demand` before reading it as progression, and always run a control that is at least as long as the arm.** #1606 / #1945. |
| 86 | A **generic-named shell redirect into the shared `~/` scratch**, read as writing a private file — trap 19's other half, on the *write* side | Trap 19 covers *reading* a peer's data, and its saving grace was the Write tool **refusing to clobber a file it had not read**. A shell redirect has no such guard: `> ~/babylon-work/build.log` truncates whatever was there, silently, exit 0. The Asterix lane found `~/babylon-work/` **already populated by an earlier lane on the same title** (a dozen `pr*.md` / `issue-*.md` from two days before) and had by then written `cmake-configure.log`, `build.log` and `build2.log` into it — three names any lane on any title would pick. Whether those overwrote that lane's files is **unknowable after the fact**, and that is the point: the loss is undetectable from both sides, and the victim meets it as a log that is missing or wrong during a re-run they cannot explain. Only the lane's `babylon-*`-prefixed artifacts were safe, and they were safe by construction rather than by care. **Two rules. Prefix EVERY file a lane writes under `~/` with its lane or title — including configure and build logs, which feel like throwaways and are exactly the names that collide. And a scratch directory you did not create is a peer's workspace: `ls -la` it before writing, because finding it populated is the only warning you will get.** Same family as #19, with the asymmetry that matters: the tool that asks permission is the protected one, so the plain `>` that feels lighter is the dangerous one. |
| 87 | A **diagnostic that prints only on its failure branch**, read as a verdict on the whole run | `[render] … Vulkan render FAILED` (`live_renderer.cpp:5936`) has **no success counterpart** unless frame dumps are enabled, so a log containing 946 failures and nothing else is byte-identical to "946 failures, then 1,100 silent successes". Sonic Frontiers publishes nothing for exactly its first 946 present callbacks and then publishes continuously, deterministically, in both independent arms — the earlier intake sampled entirely inside that silent prefix (all six samples by t=30 s; the first composited frame lands at t=50 s) and recorded rung 0 for a title that renders a full 3D opening sequence. Before quoting a failure census, check whether success is even printable. Cousin of trap 39. #1891, #1968. |
| 88 | **`pgrep -x <frontend> \| head -1` used to find "my" process**, then attached to with gdb — it returns the OLDEST match on the box, which on a shared machine is a **peer lane's run** | The rule above ("count with `pgrep -x`") is about *counting*; this is the *selection* mistake behind it, and it fails silently in the worst possible way. The Little Nightmares III lane took an all-thread `guest_bt` dump this way and got a coherent, plausible 64-thread UE4 backtrace — thread names, sensible unwinds, a `pthread_rwlock_wrlock` block that looked exactly like a root cause. It was **another title's process**. The tell was not in the dump but outside it: reading the LN3 PID's own `/proc/<pid>/task/*/comm` gave a completely different thread-name set (`TaskGraphThread`x39 / `RenderThread 1`, not `CRI MPV Worker` / `RenderThread 0`). Two arms and the "root cause" they produced were void, and three gdb attaches froze a peer's live run for a few seconds each. Nothing in the dump can reveal this — every title under prosper maps its eboot at the same base, so `guest_bt`'s own module map resolves and unwinds happily against the wrong binary. **Pin the PID to your own child (`pgrep -P "$WRAPPER" -x <frontend>`) AND assert `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` contains your title id before attaching; refuse to attach otherwise, so a mis-target fails loudly instead of producing evidence.** A debugger attach is also a write, not a read: it stops a peer's process. #1962. |
| 89 | **A global "is a present surface registered?" (`present_width() != 0`) used as the predicate for the per-submit question "is this frame going to the publish gate?"** | #1986 needed the live renderer to refuse a rendered pass whose extent the publisher cannot accept — but only on the present path, because `render_submit_items` deliberately returns the last pass **at its own declared extent** and its consumers (gpu_replay's ordered-prefix inspection, #1330; the renderer's own tests, #526) infer the extent from the returned byte count afterwards. `present_width()`/`present_height()` look like exactly the right discriminator: they are non-zero only when a scanout exists, and `hle_agc.cpp:1703` derives the present `w`/`h` from them. They are wrong, and they are wrong **in the tests specifically**: `tests/misc/test_gpu_capture_render.cpp` registers a **real 128x128 VideoOut present surface** at line 87 to simulate a reduced-resolution render, so the predicate is true inside the very test asserting *"per-target chain returns the last pass at its declared 16x16 extent"* — a gate keyed on it changes what that chain returns. **A global proxy for a scoped fact is true in exactly the places a test has set the global up, which is to say in exactly the places you were relying on it to be false.** "Is a present surface registered?" is global and durable; "is this submit going to the publish gate?" is per-submit and scoped, and no amount of care about the first answers the second. The discriminator that works is the reusable part: a **counted thread-local scope opened by the caller that actually presents** (`PresentSubmitScope`, opened by `execute_ordered_and_present`/`execute_and_present`), because it is true only on the path it names and a test can enter it explicitly to assert both arms. Unlike most of this list, this one **failed loudly** — a named check went red and named the wrong predicate before it could ship — and it is worth recording *why*, because it is buildable on purpose: that test asserts a **declared extent** rather than merely "some pixels came back", so it can detect a gate that silently changes what the chain returns. Contrast the defect the same PR fixes, where a wrong-extent frame was dropped for 31 consecutive submits with nothing logging it at all. Assert the specific property, not liveness. #1986 / #1990. |
| 90 | **`frame_seq` read as a liveness oracle** — it was one only as an accident of the bug #1990 fixed, and that PR removes the property while leaving the habit | Master's frozen publish counter was a genuinely useful health signal for Sonic Frontiers: `frame_seq` stuck at 2,081 meant the screen was stuck, because a frame that could not be published was not published at all. #1990 gives the renderer an extent contract and a retained-frame fallback, so the same title now publishes **~4,000 frames post-wall with an unchanging image** — `frame_seq` climbs to 5,499 while every sample carries `distinct_rgb_colors == 1` and the identical `pixel_crc32`. "The counter is climbing" no longer means "the image is alive", and the orchestrator had already drawn exactly that inference once before the numbers were in. **The general rule is about the aftermath of any fix, not about this counter: when a fix removes a failure mode, ask which signals you were reading as health were only side effects of that failure — a fix can silently retire an oracle, and nothing warns you, because the oracle keeps returning values.** Pair a counter with a **content** metric before calling a title alive: `distinct_rgb_colors`, `nonblack_rgb_pixels`, and above all a **differing `pixel_crc32` per sample**. The Bendy control in the same PR is the shape to copy — 15 samples, a *different* hash on each, which is liveness; the Frontiers arm has a climbing counter and one repeated hash, which is not. And when the distinction itself is the question, instrument the branches rather than inferring: #1990's `fresh=`/`retained=` totals showed fresh growing by 1 while retained grew by 2,048, which no counter reading could have separated. Cousin of trap 87 (a signal that cannot express the state you care about) and of #1990's own defect. #1990. |
| 91 | **A pixel-inspection metric that hard-codes one pixel format** — `PROSPER_PASS_LOG`'s `px_nonblack` counted 4 bytes per pixel and tested bytes `p`, `p+1`, `p+2` for *every* render target | This does not degrade on another format; it **inverts**. On Sonic Origins' 3840x2160 `R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT` scene target (8 bytes per texel) the loop walked half-texels, reading `{R_lo, R_hi, G_lo}` and then `{B_lo, B_hi, A_lo}`, and reported **8,294,400 non-black pixels — exactly `w*h`** for a frame with no colour in it at all. Maximum brightness for black: the failure is indistinguishable from the best possible result, so nothing about the number invites a check. It briefly became this lane's root cause ("content is rendered and the present path drops it") and was killed only by a second instrument, the `PROSPER_DUMP_PERSISTENT` census, which converts properly and reported black. **The mechanism is worth reading closely, because the obvious explanation is also wrong**: it is *not* "alpha 1.0 is non-zero". An alpha of exactly 1.0 (`0x3c00`) would have counted **zero**, since its only non-zero byte is the `p+3` the loop skipped. The measured target holds RGB bit-zero with alpha `0x3c05` (1.00488), whose low mantissa byte `0x05` sits at texel byte 6 — the second group's `p+2`. So the count tracked *where a half-float happened to put its bytes*, not the image; the same buffer with a rounder alpha reads 0, and an independent review caught the plausible story before it reached the record. Same family as the seed-miss gradient scoring more distinct colours than real content, and as a draw census without a positive control. **The fix, and it generalises:** Convert through the format's own inspection path before counting anything (#1994 routes `px_nonblack` through `inspection_rgba8`, the converter the persistent dump already used). Where no conversion exists, report **`-1`**, not a number: an honest refusal is a signal a reader can act on and a plausible number is not — and print that case *more* readily than a zero, because it is the one where the instrument cannot answer. Whenever a metric is fixed this way, name the past conclusions that rested on it; a silently corrected instrument leaves live wrong conclusions behind it. |
| 92 | **A startup-timing number taken without recording the host page-cache state** — the same binary, on the same host, is 2-4x apart depending only on whether the guest's assets are already resident | A guest `read`/`pread` is served from the **host page cache** on every launch after the first, so a title's asset load runs at memory speed rather than storage speed and *nothing in the run says which happened*. On PPSA26414 that is not a rounding error, it decides the outcome: the INPUT-thread-to-`DLLInit` interval is **289-362 ms warm and 779-1158 ms with the dump evicted**, and the title faults at `eboot+0x24055` in five of five warm arms and boots in five of five cold ones. It invalidated a published figure: #2009's *"~17 ms of file I/O inside a 244 ms load"* (`strace -f -c`) is true of a **warm cache**, not a property of prosper — the same load spends 552-719 ms when the reads reach storage. Note what makes the confound nasty here: CPU contention is the *other* lever on the same interval, so a cold-cache pass reads exactly like a contention pass. The discriminator is that the **least-loaded** arms land on opposite sides — warm at load 13.4 faulted, cold at load 14.7 booted. **Record the cache state with every startup/boot timing, and make it an input rather than a hidden variable**: `tools/dropcache.py` evicts a named tree with `posix_fadvise(POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED)` and prints `mincore(2)` residency before and after, so a "cold arm" can prove it was cold instead of asserting it. And treat that tool as **shared state, not a local one**: the page cache is global, so evicting a dump evicts it for every process on the box — on 2026-08-05 six evictions of the R-Type dump silently slowed a concurrent lane running the same title, which could have been read as a regression. Announce an eviction of a shared dump before you do it. #1746 / #2019. |
| 93 | **A `gh`/`distrobox` invocation that hangs to timeout, read as "no results"** — on this box `gh` is a **distrobox wrapper script** (`exec distrobox-enter -n my-distrobox -- /usr/bin/gh`), so every invocation is a container exec | Under podman contention from concurrent lanes the lookup fails with `Error: beginning transaction: database is locked`; distrobox then concludes the container does not exist and falls back to an **interactive `Create it now? [Y/n]` prompt** that a non-interactive run cannot answer. The call hangs until its `timeout`, exits 124/143, and leaves an **empty output file** — which reads exactly like "the command produced no output", i.e. like a *negative result*. It cost several lanes time on 2026-08-05, and the same failure was first misdiagnosed as a shell tilde-expansion bug because it is intermittent and appears cwd-dependent. The unauthenticated REST API is the reliable substitute for status queries (`curl -s https://api.github.com/repos/OWNER/REPO/commits/<sha>/check-runs`) — **but it has its own instance of the same rule**: at 60 requests/hour it answers **HTTP 403** with a body that has no `check_runs` key, so a poller doing `d.get('check_runs', [])` computes "0 failures" from an error and reports **ALL SETTLED, failures: NONE** for a PR whose builds were still running. **A timeout, an empty file, or a missing field is never a negative result.** Any gate over such output must demand *positive* evidence — a non-empty response, the expected key present, and a **non-zero** count of checks that equals the number expected — rather than scanning for badness and concluding its absence is health. Check `x-ratelimit-remaining` before believing an unauthenticated answer. #2019. |
| 94 | **An existence check standing in for a capability check** — a guard that asks "is the tool installed?" when the question is "can the tool do the thing I need?" | `tools/re/nid_gate_scan.py` disassembles a raw byte window with GNU `objdump -b binary`. Its ctest skipped when `objdump --version` failed — which is never, because **macOS ships LLVM's objdump as plain `objdump`**: it answers `--version` perfectly and rejects `-b` outright (`error: unknown argument '-b'`). The guard therefore passed on the one platform where every decode raises, and the macOS CI job went red at 156/157. **Probe the capability you actually need, not the artifact that usually provides it** — here, run a real one-byte disassembly and require both exit 0 *and* the expected `nop` in the output, then accept `$OBJDUMP` / `objdump` / `gobjdump` so the tool works with Homebrew binutils rather than merely failing more politely. Two traps sit behind this one. First, it is the same family as *a test that passes without its mechanism ever running*: an existence check is a proxy that correlates with the property right up until a second implementation of the same command name appears. Second — and this is the part that nearly shipped — **a skip guard can itself become the thing that passes without running**: the first fix skipped the *whole* test file, yet 12 of its 26 assertions (`canon`, `arg0_before`) need no `objdump` at all, so macOS would have reported `re_nid_gate_classifier` green while testing nothing. Split the guard so the capability-free assertions always run, and express classifier cases as decoded tuples so the logic under test is exercised even where the decoder is unavailable. An independent review caught the second half; CI caught the first. #2021. |
| 95 | **A queued container job leaves the previous run's artifact in place**, so a stale result is indistinguishable from a fresh one | The mutation-arm table for #2021 was re-run after its test grew two checks. `distrobox enter` sat in the podman lock queue (trap 93) for minutes without starting, so the script's `: > "$OUT"` had not executed and **the previous run's file was still on disk, complete, well-formed, and two hours old**. It listed six arms instead of seven and the *old* nine check names — a result that looks exactly like a finished run, because it is one, just not this one. It was caught by `ls --time-style` on a hunch, not by anything in the output. **Check the mtime of any artifact you are about to read as a result, and compare it against when you started the run**; better, write each run to a fresh per-run path (or `mv` the old one aside before launching) so a stale file cannot occupy the name a new result is expected at. The general form: **an artifact's existence says nothing about which run produced it**, and truncate-on-start gives no protection when the start itself is what is delayed. Same family as trap 93 — absence and staleness both present as ordinary data — and as trap 20, where an absent signal mimicked a pending one. Note the asymmetry that makes this expensive: the stale file is *more* believable than a missing one, so it invites no check at all. #2021. |
| 96 | **A run that never reached the code under test emits zero diagnostic lines** — which is indistinguishable from the diagnostic looking and finding nothing | `PROSPER_AVPCHROMA_LOG` was used to A/B whether #2032's `AvPlayerVideoEx.width` change moved R-Type Delta's NV12 plane extents. Three of four "after" arms printed **no `[avpchroma]` lines at all** — which reads exactly like the honest negative you are hoping for ("the changed clause is never reached, so nothing moved"). It was not: the guest died at frame 34 on the #1746 startup race, hundreds of frames before the movie, so the instrument was never given anything to report. **The adverse condition is the one that looks ideal.** #1746 is decided by host CPU speed, and the `tools/dropcache.py` route (#2019) is called deterministic because it *wins* that race — but it wins it by making the asset load slow. On a **quiet** box prosper finishes the load well inside the guest's 400 ms window, the shell runs first and dereferences an empty user vector, so the route is **less** reliable at load ~2 than at load ~20. Every instinct that says "measure on an idle machine" is wrong here. **The tell is a content signal, not the absence of output**: `source-distinct=1` in the screenshot manifest, and a frame counter stuck at 34 against 2,248 in the arm that worked. That is the reusable part — a run that stalls still produces a well-formed manifest, a `status=ok`, ten files and an exit code of 0. General rule: **when a diagnostic's output volume depends on the guest reaching a state, its silence is only evidence once an independent, POSITIVE liveness witness from the same run says the state was reached.** Quote that witness beside the zero, or the zero is void rather than negative. Same family as trap 93 — absence presenting as ordinary data — with the extra sting that here the void arm is produced by the *better* machine conditions. **Half of this is now instrumented away, and only half:** since #2007, a run whose **primary guest thread dies** reports `guest=faulted status=GUEST-FAULT` and exits non-zero, so the specific "well-formed manifest, `status=ok`, exit 0" phrasing above no longer describes a crashed guest. A guest that *stalls* — alive, not advancing — still produces exactly that, so the content witness (`source-distinct`, the frame counter) remains the check. #2032, #2019, #1746, #2007. |
| 97 | **gdb records a longjmp landing value as a function's return value** — so a capture is not merely incomplete, it is confidently wrong, and the completeness counter says it is fine | `tools/hle_calls --values` arms a `gdb.FinishBreakpoint` per call to record what each HLE handler returned. On a handler that longjmps back into its own caller, that breakpoint sits at an address **the landing pad reuses**, so gdb captures the value present at the landing and reports it as the return. Measured on gdb 17.2 with a fixture longjmping on one call in three: the row printed `ret 0x80960007 x13, 0x0 x7` with a **complete-looking 20/20 capture**. What a reader would otherwise conclude is the expensive part — the row says prosper answered the guest `0x0` seven times, i.e. it names a specific wrong HLE answer that never happened, and that is exactly the class of finding these censuses are run to discover. It would have been chased as a mismodelled Sony return. **The per-row `(captured N/M)` field, which exists precisely to flag lost values, reads 20/20 and confirms the lie**: the values were not lost, they were *substituted*. General rule: **a completeness counter measures presence, never provenance** — it cannot distinguish "the value I recorded belongs to this call" from "I recorded a value". Any capture keyed on a code address is open to this wherever control flow can re-enter that address by a path other than a normal return: longjmp, signal return, exception unwinding, a reused tail-call landing pad. The only check that caught it was the **source-derived positive control** — `s_user_getevent` returns `0` exactly once per process and `0x80960007` forever after, known from `hle_service.cpp` before any run — which flagged `positive-control=VIOLATED(login-x7)` while every other field in the output looked healthy. Pair every value capture with a control whose answer you derived from the source beforehand; nothing computed from the capture itself can detect substitution. #2035, #1997, #2054. |
| 98 | **A mutation arm implemented as "stash the file and re-run"** — void the moment the change under test is committed, and it reports every check *passing* | `git stash push -- <path>` removes only *uncommitted* changes to that path. Once the fix is committed the stash removes nothing, the rebuild is byte-identical, and the arm reports 3-of-3 green. Nothing in the output distinguishes that from a genuinely non-discriminating test — the procedure, the build and the exit code are all unchanged. The failure direction is what makes it expensive: it fails toward *"my test doesn't discriminate"*, which an author is **pleased to disbelieve** and which a reviewer cannot see from the diff at all. Caught on #2061 only because the same arm had failed **2-of-3 an hour earlier** and then passed **3-of-3 after the test was made stricter** — a discriminator that gets better and starts passing is the signature. **Mutate the source in place** (neutralise the exact lines) rather than relying on VCS state, and **require the arm to show at least one failure**: an arm that cannot show its lever moved is *no result*, not a negative one. Note the defence here was the author disbelieving an implausible improvement in their own result, not review — review cannot see it. #2061. |
| 99 | **A fault reporter that is not reentrant** — the fatal worker-fault dump kept the interrupted context in *process globals* (`g_fault_addr`, `g_fault_rip`, `g_rax`…`g_r15`), so a second thread faulting mid-dump rewrote them underneath the report already printing | The guest's faults are correlated: one heap corruption takes several worker threads within milliseconds. The first thread's report then continued with the *second* fault's registers and still read as one complete fault — right thread name on every line (`pthread_getname_np(pthread_self())` is per-thread), wrong numbers beside it. On Crisis Core one report printed `rbp` as `0x7fe46d761280` in its header and `0x7fe4603ff3a0` sixty bytes later, `rsp` likewise, and `fs(rax)=0x0` against a ucontext `rax` of `0x30016000`; another printed two `guest fp-chain` blocks that were **both** `PoolThread 6`'s, one of them under the RHIThread's header. The tell was a self-contradiction nobody had cashed: `insn bytes @rip` decoded to `mov eax,[rbx]` with `rbx = 0x20`, which cannot fault at the `0x30016000` in its own header — the bytes were fetched at another thread's rip. It reached further than the prose: #1992 had widened `[faultobj]`'s probe-page list *specifically* so #1945's corrupted pointer would not be missed, and that list was built from the same globals, so under a concurrent fault it scanned pages derived from another thread's registers and still printed `probe-pages=N/M` — making a zero-hit ring or clock-fence result **void rather than negative**, the exact failure #1992 existed to remove. **The general rule is the expensive part: a diagnostic that is not itself reentrant misreports most severely exactly when the bug is worst.** A single-fault program never exposes it, every unit test passes, and the one situation that breaks it — a racing multi-thread fault — is the one where you most need the dump to be trustworthy, so the instrument degrades precisely as the subject gets harder. Ask of any handler-resident diagnostic: what happens if this runs twice at once? A report is also cheaply self-checking — **the same register printed twice in one dump must agree**, and any that does not invalidates everything between the two prints. Same family as trap 12, where two interleaved streams read as one ordered sequence. Fixed by #2030 (a stack-local per-fault snapshot plus a single-owner gate on the report); #2018. |
| 100 | **`pidof <frontend>` returns EVERY concurrent lane's process**, and an attach-by-PID instrument then produces a complete, well-formed histogram **of the wrong game** | Trap 88 is the same family for `pgrep -x … \| head -1` + `guest_bt`; this is the `pidof` spelling, and it fails through a *tool* rather than a hand-written attach, which changes what the tell looks like. The CrossWorlds lane ran `tools/hle_calls --pid $(pidof screenshot \| head -1)` during a phase it wanted to characterise and got 47 distinct handlers, 15,950 calls, a sensible AGC/audio/VideoOut mix and a plausible positive control — a result with **no internal defect of any kind**, because it was a perfectly good measurement of *Sonic Origins* running in another worktree. Three lanes were on `screenshot` at that moment; `pidof` returns them all, in an order nobody should rely on. Note the shape, because it is the worst one a false positive can take: an *empty* histogram would have been questioned immediately, and a *malformed* one would have been debugged. A complete one is simply believed, and every number in it is then reasoned about as if it described your title. The tell was outside the data, in the tool's own header line naming the binary it enumerated (`hle_calls: 763 handler symbols in …/worktrees/origins3/build/screenshot`) — which is exactly why `hle_calls` prints it, and exactly the line a reader skips on the way to the numbers. **The discriminator is one command and it is title-independent: `readlink /proc/<pid>/exe` must equal your own build's binary path.** Assert it, do not eyeball it — with several worktrees the paths differ by one directory component. Trap 88's stronger form (`pgrep -P "$WRAPPER"` to pin the PID to your own child, plus a `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` title-id check) is better still where you control the spawn; the `exe` check is the one that works when you do not. Cousin of the counting rule under *The environment is part of the system under test* — `ps \| grep` and `pgrep` mislead about **how many**, this misleads about **which one**, and only the second silently answers a question about someone else's title. #1895 / #2013. |
| 101 | **A one-frame headless F9 grab reporting `window had no submits` is a fact about the WINDOW, not about the title** | The `PROSPER_CAPTURE_BUNDLE_TRIGGER_FILE` grab arms on the next present and closes on the one after, so its window is exactly **one present interval**. On CrossWorlds it fired at present 470 and reported `[grab] frame-bundle: window had no submits; press F9 again`. Read as a statement about the title — "it has stopped submitting GPU work, that is why the composite is empty" — it is a diagnosis, and it is wrong. The counter-evidence is direct and was already available: `PROSPER_SUBMITLOG_DIM=3840x2160` over the *same phase* counts **53 submits to the 4K present extent and 89 draw items per 10 s**. The title presents faster than it submits, so a randomly-placed one-present window legitimately catches zero, and the grab's message is an accurate report of an empty window rather than a defect. **Any title whose submit rate is below its present rate makes the default single-frame grab a coin flip, and nothing in the message says which kind of zero you got.** Fix: `PROSPER_CAPTURE_FRAMES=N` (1..240) widens the window to N presents; pick N from a measured submit-per-present ratio rather than a guess, and treat a still-empty wide window as the interesting result. The general form is worth more than the mechanism: a diagnostic whose scope is one sampling unit reports the *unit's* emptiness, and turning that into a claim about the subject requires a second measurement at the subject's own timescale. Cousin of trap 90 — there a counter climbed while nothing changed; here nothing was counted while plenty changed. #2013. |
| 102 | **A single sample from an arm that carried an invasive instrument is not a measurement of the subject** — and the invasive part need not be running when the sample is taken | The CrossWorlds lane published "the uniform post-logo frame is a phase, not a terminal state" — in the status doc, the game tracker *and* a PR body — on the strength of **one** sample: the final t=270 s frame of a 270 s arm read pure black instead of the uniform colour it had held since t=90 s. It does not reproduce. A clean 900 s arm is uniform at **all 30** samples from t=90 s to t=900 s, and a second 270 s arm at the same cadence is uniform at t=270 s. The arm that produced the outlier had `gdb` attached twice, at t≈120 s and t≈170 s, for `hle_calls` and a backtrace — both finished long before the sample, which is precisely why the confound was not felt: **an attach stops every thread and resumes them in an order the guest never chose, and a UE5 frame loop that survives that can still have taken a different branch.** "The instrument was not running when I measured" is not the same as "the instrument did not affect the run". What makes this worth a row rather than a note is the reflex it needs, which is cheap and general: **before publishing an outlier, re-run the arm with the instrument removed.** One clean arm settles it, costs one run, and is the only thing that distinguishes a phase transition from an apparatus artifact. Two further notes from the cleanup. First, the surprising direction is the dangerous one — the black sample was *more* interesting than the truth (progression rather than a wall) and that is what made it publishable; see trap 46 for the same asymmetry. Second, **a retraction has to reach every place the claim reached.** This one was in three documents, and retracting it in one would have left two live copies that the next agent has no way to know are dead — the status doc keeps a `## Ruled out` row saying so explicitly rather than deleting the original line. #1895 / #2013. |
| 103 | **A diagnostic gated on a hard-coded address window goes silently inert when the addresses move, and its silence is indistinguishable from "the thing did not happen"** | `PROSPER_MB3WATCH`'s arm hook filtered on `[0x20..0x21)` while **every current title's cache base is `0x30xxxxxxxx`**, so it armed nothing, on any title. Every null it produced before #2060 is **void, not negative** — including nulls that were quoted as evidence. This is the third inert-instrument discovery in two days: #1998 (`PROSPER_WATCH_LABEL`/`WATCH_HOT` gated on a DOLL-era window excluding all four titles it was written for) and the `{rdx, rax}`-only fault probe fixed in #1992 are the others, which makes it a class rather than an accident. The cheap defence is that **an arm hook must report whether it armed** — `armed=0` and `fired=0` are different facts and only one of them is a measurement. Before quoting any zero from a windowed diagnostic, confirm the window still contains the subject. #2060 / #2080. |
| 104 | **A read-only probe whose *duration* decides whether the subject lives** — "it only observes" is exactly the wrong reassurance for a timing-decided failure | `PROSPER_MB3_POISON` mutates nothing in the guest and is still a confound on `PPSA07809`, because #1945 there is decided by **how long the guest's own submit call takes**: 12 arms, 4 doses, one binary differing only in a `nanosleep` — **0/3 survive at none, 0/3 at 500 µs, 3/3 at 1500 µs, 3/3 at 3000 µs** (Fisher two-tailed p ≈ 0.002). So a diagnostic that changes nothing observable can still change the outcome, purely by costing time. Read-only is not the same as non-invasive. Before trusting a result taken under a read-only probe on a title whose failure is timing-sensitive, **measure the probe's own cost and re-run without it**. Note this does NOT revive "the guest outruns prosper" — that reading is separately falsified on ArcRunner, where `PROSPER_RENDER=0` faults at the same site with the same value, sooner. The per-title dose-response for ArcRunner is #2084. #2060 / #2080. |
| 105 | **A per-handler call census that silently omits a whole class of handler** — its zero for the omitted class reads as a measured "the guest never calls this" | `tools/hle_calls` enumerates handlers out of the binary by their six-`unsigned long` HLE signature; on Sonic Origins (#1905) it reported `armed=153` and a clean, complete-looking histogram. **`s_np_check_cb_entry` and `s_netctl_check_cb_entry` are not among those 153** — they are `PROSPER_ASM_TRAMPOLINE` entries rather than C++ handlers, so the signature walk cannot see them and they are never armed. Their zero is not "these were not called", it is "these were not looked at", and nothing in the output distinguishes the two. What that invites is specific, plausible and actionable: the title registers an NP state callback and a NetCtl callback during boot, so a census showing neither `CheckCallback` ever ran says **the guest never pumps its callbacks, and prosper's SIGNED_OUT / DISCONNECTED events are therefore never delivered** — a coherent boot-stall mechanism for a title that renders an empty scene forever, and one this lane wrote down before falsifying it. It is false: the same run's `PROSPER_SVCLOG` carries `[svc] Np state callback DELIVERED (userId=1, state=SIGNED_OUT, guest_fs=1)` and `[svc] NetCtl state callback DELIVERED (eventType=DISCONNECTED, guest_fs=1)`. General rule: **a census reports absence for whatever its enumeration step excluded, and the exclusion never appears in the result** — `armed=N` counts what was armed, never what should have been, so it reads as a completeness figure while being silent on coverage. Before believing a zero from any census, confirm the thing you are asking about is in the enumerated set (here: `nm build/boot_trace \| grep s_np_check_cb` against the tool's own armed list), and settle the question with a second instrument that reaches it by a different route — the `[svc]` log was that instrument, and it needed no cooperation from the census. Same family as trap 102, where `pidof` selected another lane's process: both are censuses that are complete-looking and wrong, and in both the output's own summary field is what makes them believable. #1905, #2076. |
| 106 | **A misspelled environment variable is indistinguishable from a feature that ran and reported nothing** | `avp_log()` reads `PROSPER_AVPLOG` (`src/hle/service/hle_service.cpp:978`). Two ArcRunner arms passed `PROSPER_AVP_LOG=1` — one underscore — and reported `video-ex calls: 0`, which was read as "the movie never started" and nearly became a finding about the guest. Re-run with the correct spelling, the same arms report **31**. `getenv` returning NULL is silent by construction, so a typo produces a confident zero with no diagnostic anywhere. Generalises past this variable: **an env-gated diagnostic that finds nothing must be made to say so** (`ARMED` on the way in, or a population counter on the way out), or its silence is unattributable. Before quoting any `PROSPER_*` zero, `grep` the exact string in `src/` and confirm something reads it. |
| 107 | **A documented diagnostic that is inert in the configuration the document prescribes** | `docs/ARCRUNNER_STATUS.md`'s reproduction block told the next agent to run `PROSPER_PRESENT_NZLOG=1` under `boot_trace`. Alone it emits **zero** `[render-nz]` lines over ~100 presented frames: the line is gated on `!px.empty()` in `frontends/shared/live/live_renderer.cpp`, `px` comes from `selected_pixels`, and the registrar announces `dump=0` because the readback that fills it is opt-in and this switch does not request it. The variable exists and is read — so trap 106's grep passes — it simply cannot fire without a companion. A recipe is not evidence that a recipe works. **Re-run a prescribed command and check it produces output before trusting a result taken with it**, and when correcting one, fix the recipe itself and not only the prose warning 300 lines away. |
| 108 | **A rate limiter working exactly as designed makes a RARE class invisible when it shares an ordinal with a common one** | The sharpest of this group, because nothing is broken. `PROSPER_LIVEPTR_TRIP` printed on the standard schedule — first 64, then powers of two — off one global counter. ArcRunner produces hundreds of free-list-residue hits for every one module-image vptr hit, so the vptr landed at ordinal ~300: past the first-64 window, not a power of two, **counted in the totals and never printed**. The run that mattered was thrown away and the arm re-run. The limiter did its job; the sampling was simply not stratified. **Key a diagnostic's print schedule per CLASS, not globally, whenever one class is rare and another is common** — the first hit of every new class must always print. The same failure returns at the bucket-table ceiling, so a nonzero "other" bucket means *raise the table and re-run*, never *no further classes*. |
| 109 | **`snprintf`'s return value passed to a raw `write(2)` — a real out-of-bounds read, not merely a wrong count** | Not an instrument that lied so much as one that corrupted its own evidence, and it belongs here because it degrades on exactly the richest lines.
