Nothing was lost only because **both** agents happened to recognise foreign content and recover by hand (`git cat-file -t` on the detached commit, then `git stash store`); a dropped stash commit is unreachable and GC-eligible, so the silent version of this loses another lane's work with no trace and mixes two issues into one branch. *Recognition cue:* **a `pop` whose diff touches a file you never edited** -- and, more generally, any shared repository state that the worktree rule is quietly assumed to cover. *Pinned:* the first arm of `tools/test_wt_stash.py` builds two linked worktrees by hand and asserts the collision still reproduces, so every later arm has a positive control. *Fix:* `tools/wt_stash.py` parks under `refs/worktree/prosper-stash/<slot>`, which `git-worktree(1)` documents as not shared, addressed by name rather than by top-of-stack and refusing an occupied slot instead of stacking. An alias cannot shadow `git stash` (measured), so the rule is written, not enforced. #3174 |
| 248 | **A watchdog whose verdict covers ONE 256-byte window, read -- and published -- as a verdict about a multi-megabyte range** | #3142, 2026-09-02. `PROSPER_ZEROWATCH` arms on the first non-zero 256-byte window of an APR payload and polls *that window*. Its line reads `dst=0x… size=13674533 … WENT-ZERO`, naming the whole payload size, and the verdict was carried into an issue and into a `## Ruled out` row as "the range is zeroed" / "the data is gone". Profiling 16 windows at the instant of the flip says otherwise: **11 of 14** destinations still hold most of their payload, and still hold it 45 s later; only 3 are genuinely all-zero, and one window that read zero at the flip read non-zero again at +10 s. What the guest clears is usually the chunk *header* -- window 0 -- which is exactly the window the arming rule always picks, so the instrument was aimed at the one place that always moves. *Why it survived so long:* every surrounding measurement was sound (the write-back verifier, the shared monotonic stamp, the `/proc/self/maps` dump, the `libc.prx` attribution) and each one investigated the flip rather than its extent, so the scope error was never the thing under test. It also passed a positive control: the sampler *does* report content when content is at that window. *Fix:* the verdict line now prints a 16-window profile against the SOURCE's own per-window census, plus re-profiles at +10 s and +45 s, so "the head went" and "the range went" can never again be the same log line. *General form:* when a watchdog samples a subset and names a superset in its output, the output is the bug -- state the sampled extent in the line itself, or somebody will quote the name. Cousin of 236 (same instrument family, and the same failure of a green control to bound what was measured). **And the fix reproduced the trap inside itself, which is the part worth reading:** review of #3243 found that the new re-profile timer fires immediately on the `WENT-UNMAPPED` path, because that branch never stamps `t_reported` and `diag_now_us()` is CLOCK_MONOTONIC since boot -- so a range that vanished entirely printed `+10s` and `+45s` profiles reading `lost=0 kept=0` within 100 ms. A correction that reintroduces the shape it corrects is the normal outcome, not a surprising one; budget a review for it. |
| 249 | **A ctest per-test DURATION on the macOS/Rosetta runner measures process startup, not the test -- so a failing run reads FASTER than a passing one, and reasoning from the duration inverts the sign** | #3067 lane, 2026-09-02. `kernel_sem_timedwait` failed twice at **1.02 s** and **1.22 s**, and an issue comment reasoned from those figures to ARM 4 (a posted acquire granted a one-second timeout) waiting that timeout out -- a plausible chain, offered honestly as a lead. It is arithmetically impossible: **every** test in that job that links `prosper_core` pays ~1.4 s for process start plus `register_builtin_hle()` under binary translation (`kernel_nanosleep` 1.48 s, `hle_functions_registered` 1.48 s, `json2_hle` 1.46 s), against **0.01 s** for the same test on Linux. Five green `main` jobs the same day put the PASSING duration at 1.08/1.20/1.35/1.47/1.52 s -- so 1.22 s sits inside the passing distribution, 1.02 s is below its minimum, and an ARM 4 that really ran to its deadline would have read ~2.4 s. The duration is ~99% constant overhead and carries no signal about the wait at all. **The answer was in the job's own log the whole time** -- `[FAIL] and returned inside one winpthreads tick of the request, not after it` / `(timeout took 13.58 ms via none)` -- which is the general rule: when a test prints per-arm output, read the output, and treat the harness's summary line as a scheduling statistic about the runner. |
| 250 | **A test binary that ASSERTS a capability it does not have -- so the one setting that exercised the mechanism crashed there and nowhere else, and the crash was read as a property of the emulator** | #3156 / #3155, 2026-09-02. `guest_write_watch_set_fault_onstack(true)` is a promise to the watch layer: a SIGSEGV handler is installed, on a sigaltstack, and it will service a write fault on an armed page. `test_game_compute.cpp` made that promise in **seven** places while installing **no handler at all**, and compensated by calling `guest_write_watch_notify_host_write()` at the write sites it knew about -- which works exactly as long as nothing arms a watch the test did not anticipate. At `PROSPER_COMPUTE_WRITE_WATCH_PROMOTE_HITS=1` the compute cache arms on a first acquisition, the test's own `large_result[0] ^= 0xffffffffu;` stored into a page that had just been made read-only, and the process died: `notl 0x0(%r13)` at a page-aligned mmap address with `#0 main ()` as the entire backtrace. **The reachable conclusion from that crash is the wrong one.** It was filed as a user-facing trap in a `PROSPER_*` switch, and the sibling issue then recorded that `hits=1` -- the only value that measurably reduced `memcmp` traffic in production -- was "unusable", so the measurement everybody wanted was blocked by the harness rather than by the emulator. Production installs the handler and ran `hits=1` repeatedly without incident. *Recognition cue:* **a failure that reproduces in exactly one binary, on a code path that binary opts into by asserting a precondition about itself.** Grep for the assertion, not for the crash. *Fix:* install the one production behaviour that matters (`tests/host/test_dmem.cpp` already had the pattern), keep every other fault fatal and loud, and add an arm that takes a raw unhooked store through an armed page -- it reddens the whole suite the moment the handler goes away, at any `hits` value. A second arm forks a child onto a `PROT_NONE` page and requires it to die: a handler that swallows is worse than the crash it replaced, and without that arm nothing would notice. |
| 251 | **A diagnostic that arms itself on a precondition it never checks -- so `PROSPER_GEOM_PROBE` reported "the draw produced no primitives" about draws that produced primitives** | #3248, 2026-09-02. The probe reads a draw's post-transform positions back through `VK_EXT_transform_feedback`, which captures nothing unless the last pre-rasterization stage declares `OpExecutionMode ... Xfb`. The recompiler emits that decoration under its OWN separate gate (`capture_position` / `capture_geometry_position`), and several paths skip it -- a hand-written fixture shader, a draw reaching the generated geometry stage without the flag, any module the probe did not itself cause to be recompiled. The probe checked only that the env var was set and that the device had the extension, so on such a draw it bound transform feedback anyway (`VUID-vkCmdBeginTransformFeedbackEXT-None-04128`), read a zero counter, and printed *"transform feedback wrote 0 vertices (draw produced no primitives)"*. That is a **wrong** answer, not a missing one -- and the expensive kind, because `GEOMETRY-VANISH` is precisely the hypothesis that sends someone to this probe in the first place, so the instrument agreed with the belief that summoned it. *Recognition cue:* **a diagnostic whose null output is also its most-expected finding**; and, generally, an arming condition that names the environment variable rather than the artefact the variable was supposed to produce. Both were checked here and the two diverged. *Why nothing caught it:* the validation layer names it in one line, but `tools/vkval` observes ctest and **no test ever armed the probe**, so the VUID had nowhere to appear -- a second blind spot on top of synchronization validation being off, and one that no configuration of the guard could have fixed. The same blindness had `PROSPER_DRAW_ISO` replaying every draw's pipeline with **none** of the dynamic state the main loop sets (`VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-07831`/`-07832`), i.e. naming a culprit draw from a render that was not the one being isolated. *Fix:* the backend tests the SPIR-V words it is about to hand Vulkan and refuses to arm, loudly, when the capture is absent; one shared recorder puts the dynamic state in both loops; and `render_diagnostic_paths` runs both diagnostics once so the layer sees them from now on. **The isolation half is improved, not fixed, and the residual is exactly the shape of this trap:** the re-render still omits the per-draw `vkCmdClearAttachments` depth/stencil clear, so a submit carrying a guest depth clear is still isolated against different depth contents. Dynamic state is the part the layer can see; this part it cannot, which is why it needs writing down instead of a VUID. **Arm a new env-gated render path there in the change that adds it, or the guard cannot cover it.** |
| 252 | **The ELF program header's `p_offset` is not where a SELF segment's bytes are -- so a disassembly taken at it is off by a constant, lands mid-instruction, and reads as "these addresses are wrong"** | #2928 (review of #2924), 2026-08-22. In the eboot #2924 took its wrapper addresses from, `PT_LOAD[0]` declares `p_offset = 0x4000` while the **SELF segment table** puts that same payload at file offset **`0x8010`**. Reading the file at `p_offset` therefore shifts every address by `0x4010` and disassembles unrelated AVX code from a byte boundary that is not an instruction start -- so it produces *plausible* instructions that are not the ones at the address you asked for. The reviewer nearly filed the addresses in #2924 as wrong before catching it, which is the shape worth recognising: **the instrument does not fail, it answers a different question, and the wrong answer arrives dressed as a finding about someone else's work.** prosper's own loader has always been right here -- `src/self/module.cpp:91` reads the `SelfSegment` table at `0x20` and overwrites each phdr's `file_off` from it (`:111`) -- so the trap is confined to reading a SELF by hand with `objdump`/`readelf`/a hex editor, which know nothing about the segment table. *Cue:* addresses that are all wrong by the same amount, or a disassembly whose instruction stream looks valid but has nothing to do with the function you named. *Avoid it:* take the offset from `tools/self_dump`, or add `SELF segment file_offset - phdr p_offset` to every address before seeking. |
| 253 | **`ulimit -c 0` suppresses the core FILE but not the handler that costs the time, so it reads as a fix while the stall it was meant to cure survives** | #3269/#3306, 2026-09-03. Tests that kill children on purpose (`test_libc_alloc`, `test_raw_syscall`) each trigger `systemd-coredump`; across concurrent lanes the handlers pile up and stall an unrelated third suite. `ulimit -c 0` was the obvious remedy and is in wide use here. Measured, 20 SIGSEGVs per arm: under `ulimit -c 0` the handler is invoked **20/20** and stores **0** files (0.78 s); unlimited stores 20 (8.53 s); `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)` invokes it **0/20** (0.009 s). A pipe-prefixed `core_pattern` makes the kernel spawn the handler regardless and leaves `RLIMIT_CORE` for it to interpret, so the process spawn and journal commit are paid on every crash -- which is the stall. **The cheap check confirms the wrong thing:** crash under the flag, look in `/var/lib/systemd/coredump`, find nothing, conclude it worked. `coredumpctl list`'s COREFILE column reads `none` for exactly those entries, and an earlier draft of this row's own fix counted journal ENTRIES as dumps and so claimed the flag did nothing at all -- wrong in the other direction, and caught only because a reviewer re-ran it at 20 crashes per arm instead of one. *Recognition cue:* a mitigation whose evidence is the ABSENCE of an artifact, where the cost you care about is the work that produces it. Verify by timing or by handler invocations, never by looking for the file. |
| 254 | **A reject diagnostic that prints every provenance route EXCEPT the one that runs in the failing case -- so four fields that are null by construction were read, three times over two issues, as evidence that the descriptor was absent** | #3126 / #1634, 2026-09-03. `[mimg-unresolved]` prints five FIELDS covering four provenance routes for an image op's descriptor, and did **not** print the fifth route, `by_sgpr_base(SRSRC)`. Four of the five fields are forced, over two of the four routes: the `s_load` tag (`srt_tag`) and the SRT key (`key_res`) it gates, and the #1773 copy alias (`ud_alias`) and the `alias_res` it gates -- `sreg_srt` and `sreg_ud_alias` are populated only by `record_scalar_write`, the same function that inserts into `sreg_written`. **The remaining field, `pc_res`, is NOT forced and must not be read as if it were**: it is `by_fetch_pc`, keyed by the instruction with no register-state guard, and it is the resolver's FIRST route -- its null is a real measurement, and separating its two possible causes is what a later `PROSPER_DYNTRACE_FAIL=1` run was needed for. Getting that boundary wrong in this row's own first draft (it listed `pc_res` among the forced fields and omitted `ud_alias`) would have taught readers to discard the one null on the line that means something, which is this trap with the sign flipped -- so state the mechanism per field, never a count. The same line also prints `written`, and `written=0` means the shader never wrote the SRSRC registers -- the descriptor is entry user data by the shader's own construction, so there is no `s_load`, hence **no tag, no SRT key and no copy alias can exist**. For such a site all four printed fields are null *whatever is wrong*, and the quadruple is a restatement of `written=0` rather than a measurement. The one route that can fire is the one that was invisible -- and it was not returning null: `by_sgpr_base` is first-match-wins and class-blind, so it returned a `ConstantBuffer` sitting at exactly the requested SGPR, which the resolver then discarded on class in silence. **Three of the four `written=0` sites on Stray's title screen were like that**, and "nothing is bound at s8" and "a cbuf is bound at s8" printed the identical line. The cost is measured, not feared, and the *history* of the line is part of the argument: #1634 was filed in an era when the line printed a **triple** and carried no `written` field at all. #1634 read its own line correctly; what it could not do was tell a forced null from a measured one, because the field that separates them was not yet printed. #3126's 2026-08-30 thread then posted "FOUND IT" on it, retracted it, then wrote "the resolver correctly falls through to `by_sgpr_base(8)` -- and that is what returns null", which was never observable and is false on the vertex site; and a 2026-09-03 lane re-derived the same reading a fourth time. *Recognition cue:* **a diagnostic whose fields enumerate mechanisms, plus a field that says which mechanism applies -- check whether the enumerated ones CAN fire under the stated mode before reading their emptiness as data.** A null that a field's own preconditions force is void, exactly like a positive control drawn from the source it validates (trap 122); it is worse here only because it is printed next to a true fact (`written=0`) that explains it, so it reads as corroboration. *Fix:* the line prints `sgpr_res=<class>` and `need=<sampled\|storage\|either>`, and its `available:` list prints **every** key a resource carries -- it used to print one, `srt=0x%x` whenever `sgpr_base` was unset, so a resource keyed only by `fetch_pc` (how the const-fold publishes a seed- or table-recovered T#) read as `srt=0xffffffff`, i.e. as unkeyed; four of nine resources in the failing pixel stage were that. The lookups are class-filtered too, so a wrong-class hit can no longer shadow a usable one or suppress the fallback beneath it. **The follow-up run vindicates the instrument rather than the reading**: `PROSPER_DYNTRACE_FAIL=1` puts all five sites at `have_t8=0 seed_t8=0` against 29 `have_t8=1` and 21 `seed_t8=1` elsewhere in the same run -- a *measurement with a live discriminator*, which the four null fields never were. *And the trap kept biting after the fix:* that same run was read as "the bytes are absent, #305 window family" and then, on a second look, as "a texture descriptor consumed as a buffer" -- both withdrawn within the hour. `seed_t8=0` is a **shape** verdict, and the bytes turn out to be present, readable, and two well-formed V#s. A field named for the thing you are hunting invites you to read its zero as that thing's absence; decode the bytes it was judging before you do. #3313. |
| 255 | **Two live arms of DIFFERENT RUN LENGTHS, compared program-by-program.** A 30-frame baseline against a 12-frame arm, on Stray's title-screen route, judging which compute programs a branch skips. | **Three false readings from one comparison, in BOTH directions, and each looked like a result.** A program reported as *newly skipped* by the branch (`0x300ba70000`) was in fact newly **fixed** by it -- skipped on master at pc=3, not skipped on the branch; it had simply not been re-reached in the longer reference run. A program reported as *fixed* (`0x30131d0000`) was **unchanged**, rejecting at pc=50 in both. An `[mimg-unresolved]` "10 -> 4" improvement was **8 in both**. The sign is not predictable, so "my arm looks worse, therefore I regressed something" is as unsafe as the flattering direction: a longer run visits more state and skips more programs *and* re-reaches programs the short run never gets to. **Match frame count, route and warmup, and run the arms back to back**, or a per-program skip census is uninterpretable; a cumulative census read at the same frame ordinal is the cheap form. #3308, #3312. |
| 256 | **A new counter named `mprotect=` added to a diagnostic line that already carried `protect=`, where the OLD field counts protect FAILURES.** So `protect=0` -- the healthy value, meaning nothing failed -- sits on the same line as the new field and reads as *"zero page protections were issued"*. | #3317 / #3307, 2026-09-04. The field was added specifically to decide whether a compute writeback's `watch_ms` stage is an `mprotect` or a page-index walk, and on its **first run** the line was read as answering that with a flat no: `protect=0` was quoted as the new counter and the author's hypothesis recorded as falsified. It was the pre-existing `create_protect_failures`. The new fields sit after `physical=` and `rearms=`, past where the quote was truncated -- and they may not have been in the build at all. *What makes this expensive rather than merely embarrassing:* the misreading produced a **confident, well-argued falsification of the right question**, complete with an alternative explanation built on the other fields in the same truncated quote (`query=89938` was offered as the cost, but no query runs inside that span at all). A wrong instrument reading that yields a null is hard to challenge, because the null is what a careful person expects to find. **The line's own other fields refuted it and were not consulted: `faults=539` counts write faults on watched pages, and a page cannot fault unless something mprotected it read-only** -- so the quoted line proves protections DID happen. *Recognition cue:* a new field whose name is a superstring or near-synonym of an existing field on the same line; and any zero quoted from a long single-line diagnostic without the whole line. *Fix:* the old field is now `protect_fail=` and the new one `page_protect=<calls>/<MiB>`, so neither name contains the other. **Quote the entire line, or name the struct member rather than the printed label.** |
| 257 | **A running-total census read BEFORE the run reached the state it was meant to describe.** The line is a legitimate running total carrying its own denominator -- and that property is exactly what licensed the error. | #3307/#3320, 2026-09-04. `PROSPER_COMPUTE_BORROW_CENSUS=1` prints every 256 submits, and the instrument's own contract says *"every line is a running total carrying its own denominator, so two reports taken at different points in a run stay comparable"*. True, and it means comparable **as measurements of different intervals** -- not stationary, which is how it was read. Measured on one Stray run: at **62k** attempts the borrow reported `hit=86.2%` / `no_cache_entry=13.8%`, and the near-miss field list was `storage=3707` of `same_addr=3724` -- **99.5%, one field, decisive**. At **332k** attempts on the *same run* it reported `hit=45.7%` / `no_cache_entry=54.3%`, and the field list had become three-way with `storage` no longer even the largest: `vk_format` 56.2%, `img_dim` 55.9%, `storage` 43.8%. **The dominant field changed IDENTITY, not merely magnitude**, so the pre-registered decision rule ("a named field -> normalise that field, never a blanket relaxation") selected a different fix from the same run depending only on when it was read. The early ratios were not noise and not wrong -- they correctly described the boot, which is simply not the state anyone wanted measured. **And the confirming check failed too:** the run's single captured frame was a uniform **(63,63,63)** fill, every 16x9 cell identical, so it never reached the title screen at all -- while a `nonblack_fraction` metric scored that flat grey wash at **0.9975**, higher than the oracle's own content (trap 209's family, with a flat fill in place of a gradient). *Recognition cue:* a percentage from a periodically-printed cumulative counter, quoted without stating the run phase it covers -- and note that the denominator being present is what makes it look self-validating. *Avoid it:* take the census from the block printed **at exit**, and only after an independent artifact (an opened frame, not a scalar metric) confirms the run reached the state; if the ratios differ between two blocks in one run, the run has phases and no single number is "the" answer. |
| 258 | **An artifact's PROVENANCE read off its PICTURE — when the capture tool falls back to guest memory, a correct-looking image is the WEAKEST evidence available.** | #3332, 2026-09-04. Three artifact substitutions in one review cycle on one claim, each caught only by the next reader. (1) A reviewer established "something published" from `frame_%04d.bmp` (fed by `selected_pixels`) — a file the runs never produced; the evidence was `tools/screenshot` PNGs. (2) The author corrected it to `present_readback`, which **that tool never calls** — `screenshot.cpp:5`'s comment said it did and was stale, sending two readers independently to the same wrong function (the #2049 propagation shape). (3) The corrected sentence still read the IMAGE as proof of publication, and that conclusion was **false**: `present_snapshot` falls back to the guest's own display buffer when the renderer published nothing (`RawScanout`), and the tool saves it by default (`render_first` defaults to 0). That the two cases can be visually identical is measured elsewhere, not assumed here: `guest_scanout_present.hpp:9-17` records *Sonic Frontiers* running ~3,700 passes without a single one targeting a registered VideoOut buffer, while the buffer it flips holds "an exact SEGA logo and an exact intro shot" — a correct picture with prosper's target map empty at that address. **All three survived because nobody doubted the image; only its provenance was ever in question, and nobody asked.** The discriminator existed the whole time and needed no re-run: the manifest's `source` field (`capture_source_name()` → `composited` / `guest_scanout` / `raw_scanout`) resolved it from files already on disk — 29 of 30 captures `composited`, the eyeballed steady-state frame among them. *Recognition cue:* a claim of the form "the screenshot shows X, therefore the emulator did X", where the capture path has any fallback that can source pixels from the guest. *Avoid it:* cite the recorded provenance, never the picture; when a tool records its own source, quote that field, and prefer it to run configuration since a default can be overridden while the record cannot. And when the tool records NO source, the honest answer is that the artifact cannot establish provenance at all -- not that it probably did: reach for a capture path that does record one, or assert the fact some other way. Same family as trap 230 and the pre-rendered-frame check (`tools/evidence/prerender_check.py`): the more convincing the image, the less it discriminates. |
| 259 | **A diagnostic gated on an env var's VALUE, not its presence — `PROSPER_RENDER_TIMING=1` silently yields none of the detail the variable is named for, and the silence is indistinguishable from a resource that never took the path** | Syberia perf lane, 2026-09-05, #1811. `PROSPER_RENDER_TIMING=1` correctly turns on the phase split, and the per-texture detail line at `live_renderer.cpp:7200` — the one that names the `addr`, format, tile mode and validation verdict of every reference that MISSED its persistent cache — is gated on `strcmp(render_timing_env, "detail") == 0`. So `=1` prints the aggregate saying a mystery texture costs 26.34 ms/submit and **not one line saying which texture**, with nothing anywhere announcing that a second mode exists. A whole 240 s arm was spent that way, and the log looked complete: the aggregate line was present, correct, and the only line the reader knew to look for. Recognition cue: **an env var that this document or a status doc shows as `=1` may be a string compare rather than a boolean — grep the `getenv` before concluding the instrument looked and found nothing** (`grep -n 'getenv("PROSPER_RENDER_TIMING")'` answers it in one command). Two properties of the same detail printer belong with it, because both are read wrongly in the same breath: it is **capped at 250 lines** (`detail_lines++ < 250`), so its count is a cap and never a frequency — trap 13's family — and `PROSPER_RENDER_TIMING_DETAIL_MIN_SUBMIT` exists precisely because the cap is exhausted by the boot before the phase you care about, so an unaimed detail run reports the wrong 250 references. Generalisation, and the reason this is not just a note about one variable: **`=1` is the reader's default guess for every `PROSPER_*` switch in this repository**, so any switch whose gate is a value comparison is a silent-failure trap for everyone who has not read its source. |
| 260 | **A `grep` PATTERN that silently changes the population before the check — `grep -qaP '\xa3\x00\x6a\x80'` reported 0 matches for a constant present in 40 of 42 files.** The NUL byte terminates the pattern where the C string ends, so the search that ran was not the search that was written, and it exits 1 with no error, no warning and nothing on stderr. Trusting it would have refuted a correct central claim with an instrument artefact. Use a tool that takes binary patterns as data rather than as a C string — Python's `bytes.find` over an `mmap`, or `grep -F -f <file>` — and give any binary-pattern search a **positive control that fires**, on the same command, before believing a zero. **The general shape, because tonight produced three of these and they are worth more together:** (a) this one, 0 of 42 for something present in 40; (b) `ls -la | head -5` read as the directory's contents, which reported a 147 MB file as never created; (c) `pgrep -f "<script>"` matching 12 of the watcher's OWN shells and none of the script, stalling a chain for minutes. **No error, no warning and no exit code in any of the three.** Two answered "nothing there" and one answered "something there", so the direction is not the invariant — the invariant is that **a filter quietly changed the population before the check, and its answer then reads as a fact about the subject.** This is the same shape as *tool-filter absence is not absence*, generalised: it is not a property of censuses, it is a property of **any** filter, including the ones typed inline that nobody thinks of as instruments. A pattern, a pipe and a process matcher are all instruments. |
| 261 | **A counter's value read from `tail` of a CONCURRENTLY-WRITTEN log — `tail` orders by file position, not by value, and interleaved writers leave an early line near the end** | Syberia perf lane, 2026-09-05, #1811. Checking whether a renderer submit counter had reached an aiming threshold, `tail -3` of the run log showed `frontend submits=25` after 200 s, and the obvious conclusion — the counter is stuck at 25, the run will never arm — was published, acted on by an orchestrator, and **wrong**. `grep -o 'frontend submits=[0-9]*' \| cut -d= -f2 \| sort -n \| tail -1` gives **1925** over the same file: one monotonic counter, 77 distinct values in steps of 25, climbing at ~6.4/s. The threshold armed on schedule minutes later. *Why it survives scrutiny:* the line is real, correctly formatted, and genuinely near the end of the file — nothing is malformed, and the reader has no cue that another thread wrote it earlier. Every prosper run log is multi-threaded, so this applies to all of them. *Recognition cue:* **if you are reading a NUMBER out of a log, sort by the number; `tail` answers "what was written last", which is a different question from "what is the value now".** Sibling of the wrapped-prose row: in both cases a text tool answers a question adjacent to the one asked. *The detail that makes this row worth reading:* **the author's own run scripts still contained this exact defect while the row was being written** — three samplers built to make a run self-diagnosing, each reading its counter with a file-order `tail`. It is not an error people make from carelessness; it is one they make while documenting it. |
| 262 | **A self-diagnosing instrument can report a NOT-YET rather than a NEVER, and the two are indistinguishable at the moment you look — so a zero from your own validity check is a SAMPLE, not a verdict** | Syberia perf lane, 2026-09-05, #1811, and the more valuable half of the row above. This table is full of instruments that fail silently, so the lane had built the opposite: a sampler that counted the diagnostic's own output every 20 s, specifically so a capped 250-line printer could not report the wrong 250 references and look complete. It worked exactly as designed and printed `detail_lines=0` — **a true reading, taken minutes before the printer armed.** That was then read as a verdict about the run, producing a published retraction of a working experiment, a proposed replacement run, and a proposed instrument-trap row that was itself wrong. *The general shape:* a validity check answers "has this happened yet?", and an experimenter reads it as "will this happen?" — which is only the same question once the run is over. **A self-diagnosing instrument needs a notion of "too early to say": a zero must be qualified by whether the arming condition has been reached, or it is a countdown being read as a result.** In this instance the sampler had the answer beside it — the very submit counter the threshold is compared against — and simply did not print it. *Recognition cue:* **before believing a zero from your own instrument, check whether the thing it watches for was even possible yet.** Distinct from 145's "was the instrument armed", which asks about the instrument; this asks about the SUBJECT's progress toward the trigger. |
| 263 | **A working instrument with a BROKEN PRESENTATION LAYER: the command errors on stderr while its caller prints a hardcoded "nothing found" line on stdout, and the conclusion is the half shaped like a result.** | #2014 lane, 2026-09-05. `pgrep` accepts exactly ONE pattern, so `pgrep -x -a prosper-app screenshot boot_trace gpu_replay screenshot_snap` prints *only one pattern can be provided* and exits 2 — and the caller followed it with `echo "(no lines above = box idle)"`, which renders identically with ten titles running. It produced "no consumer at any sample" over a nine-minute window in which a peer's `screenshot` was live for two of those minutes, and nearly reported a contended box as free. *Why a positive control would NOT have caught it:* the DETECTOR was sound — a per-name `pgrep -x` loop saw the live pid correctly and every scheduling decision built on it was right. Only the REPORTING call was broken. **A control on the instrument does not cover the expression you actually quote; put the control on the quoted expression.** *The recognisable tell:* the instrument had already contradicted the conclusion IN THE SAME OUTPUT — eight `busy:` lines carrying EMPTY names, read as "nothing there". Not a missing signal; a signal rendered unreadable. *And the obvious defence is defeated:* piping the broken form to `head` swallows pgrep's exit 2 and reports exit **0**, because a pipeline's status is its LAST stage's (trap 40) — so a caller who correctly tests `$?` rather than trusting empty output still sees success. Both halves must be fixed: `set -o pipefail` / `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` / capture-then-check, AND never pair a command with a hardcoded interpretation of its empty output. *Correct spellings:* one quoted ERE is one pattern (`pgrep -x -a "screenshot\|prosper-app"`), or `ps -C a,b,c`, which does take a list. |
| 264 | **A diagnostic that prints a PLAUSIBLE CONSTANT because it reads its subject before the subject is computed — and a hypothesis retired on that constant.** | #2014 / #3113, 2026-09-05. `PROSPER_CLEARLOG` printed `rs.color0_clear_word0`/`_word1` from **two lines above** the assignments that fill them in `extract_render_state`, so it read `RenderState`'s member initializers. **Every line that diagnostic has ever printed, in every title, for every colour target, says `word0=0x00000000 word1=0x00000000`** — whatever the guest programmed. The extractor's fields were always correct, which is exactly why nothing caught it: every existing assertion on `color0_clear_word0` passed throughout the bug's whole life. *What it cost:* the strongest single piece of evidence on #2014 was built on that output — "all 33 distinct `(base, format, clear-word)` combinations report `word0=0x00000000`, so the decode cannot produce this title's colour" — and no run could have produced another number. The dedup key used the **same** unassigned field, so the 33 "distinct combinations" were 33 distinct `(base, format)` pairs with a constant third element. The family is live, not merely undecided: `decode_clear_color` maps an ALT/BGRA `COLOR_8_8_8_8` word as `r=b2, g=b1, b=b0, a=b3`, so `0x00FFFF00` decodes to exactly the `(1,1,0,0)` frame the issue is about. *Why it survived where a silent instrument would not:* traps 64, 116 and 121 are instruments that say nothing, and silence invites suspicion. This one is their loud cousin — it answers confidently, with a value that is legal, expected for most targets, and identical every time, which reads as a measurement rather than a bug. *Fix:* move the assignments above the diagnostic; and pin it by asserting the **diagnostic's text**, not the extractor's fields, since those two disagreed for the bug's entire life. Split "the instrument fired at all" from "the instrument reported the right value" into separate arms — under the mutation (move the block back above the assignments) the first deliberately does NOT move, and a single combined assertion would have hidden the zero again. *Tell:* a diagnostic whose interesting field is the same value on every line. |
| 265 | **An AGGREGATE reported on ONE key when the phenomenon is keyed on TWO — the instrument sees perfectly and answers on an axis that cannot express the distinction.** | #2014 / #1706 lane, 2026-09-05. A `--by-program` breakdown of colour-state records showed one pixel shader carrying `MODE=DISABLE` on 3,852 draws and `MODE=NORMAL` on 1,320 with its write mask unchanged, which was published as *"the decoded field does not track shader identity"* and offered as evidence that the field is stale or latched. Regrouping the SAME records by the **(vertex, pixel) pair** and the **raw register word** inverted it: `es=0x300e900000 -> cb-control=0x00cc0000` and `es=0x3016e60000 -> cb-control=0x00cc0010`, two words differing in **exactly** the bits under question and nothing else, **zero mixing across 5,054 draws**. The guest writes two distinct values per pipeline and the decode reports them faithfully — the opposite conclusion, from the same run, the same log and the same tool. *Why it is not the other traps here:* every one of those is an instrument that could not see its subject. **This one saw perfectly.** The record carried `es=` and the raw word the whole time; the aggregation key discarded them. *How it happened:* the author had just built the `--by-program` flag for this investigation, so the axis the tool reported on felt like the axis of the question — **a tool answers the question you ask, and building it yourself makes that harder to notice, not easier.** *Two rules:* before calling a DECODE unreliable, check the decoded field against the **RAW WORD** it came from — a decode disagreeing with itself and a guest writing two values look identical after aggregation; and when an aggregate looks like noise, re-key it on every identifier the record carries before concluding anything about the population. |
| 266 | **A per-draw geometry verdict that CONTRADICTS the pixels the same draw paints — and it agrees with the hypothesis that summoned it.** | #3374 lane, 2026-09-05. `PROSPER_GEOM_PROBE` re-recompiles a draw's vertex stage with transform feedback, and on a *New Joe & Mac* (`PPSA02801`) frame capsule it reported **56 of 86 draws `COLLAPSED` (all post-transform vertices at one clip point, i.e. renders nothing)**. That is exactly the answer a shear investigation wants, and a whole census, a prefix-bisect ladder and a `PROSPER_SHADER_TAP` reading were built on it before it was checked. The independent check is `--draw-steps --draw-steps-every 1`, which renders the real frame and reports what each operation actually contributes: **37 of those 56 `COLLAPSED` draws paint pixels**, and the clincher needs no statistics — `draw[86]`, a four-vertex full-screen quad that demonstrably paints **2,013,079** pixels, is reported as `3 unique positions, 100% degenerate`. So the probe's rebuild is not measuring the frame that is being investigated. This is trap 251's family at a second site: there the probe answered about draws it could not see, here it answers wrongly about draws it can. *Recognition cue:* **a per-draw verdict has a free, cheap and independent oracle — the pixels that draw contributes — and a geometry verdict that never renders is one hypothesis, not a measurement.** Two consequences beside the census. The probe's numbers were non-deterministic on `--draw a:b` sub-ranges (4, 5, 6 and 1 unique positions over 8 identical invocations) while the full-frame render was byte-identical 25 of 25 — **this row first blamed the probe for that, and that attribution was wrong**: the sub-range RENDER is itself non-deterministic on this driver (`--draw 12` gives 4 distinct output hashes in 10 runs with no probe involved, against 1 for lavapipe), so a ladder built on sub-ranges measures the driver's own dropout as much as the probe. The blindness the row is really about is the census, not the variance. And the op-to-draw mapping is **off by two**, not one — `--draw-steps`' `op<=N` is 1-based over operations and the capsule's single compute op precedes the draws, so `draw[d]` is the delta at `op<=d+2` while the probe's own `operation=` field prints `d+1`. Getting that wrong makes a correct instrument disagree with itself. *Fix used:* score every candidate draw against its `--draw-steps` contribution before quoting any geometry verdict. #3374 |
| 267 | **A renderer defect measured entirely from ONE long-lived worktree's incremental build directory — every A/B inside that tree inherits it, so nothing run there can find it.** | #3374 lane, 2026-09-05. Five rung-6 Unity titles were reported rendering their scene through large sheared triangles, measured at native scale from `.claude/worktrees/lane-fps-rung6`, a tree reused across branch switches for days. It produced a coherent, self-consistent body of evidence: 0 of 249 frames above the SSIM floor, **25 of 25 byte-identical RADV replays**, a lavapipe replay of the same capsule rendering the level correctly, and a survivable cross-implementation split that two further lanes and three rounds of review then built a mechanism for (#3375, #3377 — both merged). **None of it reproduces from a clean configure of the same commits.** Re-measured on `c4f57b1de` (two commits later, both docs or an off-by-default diagnostic): the project owner played two of the titles and saw correct pictures; ~150 `tools/screenshot` gameplay frames were opened, idle and under concurrent load with `gpu_busy_percent` measured moving 18.1%% -> 28.2%%, all correct; and `gpu_replay --bundle` on RADV — the issue's own command — rendered level 1 complete. The split itself re-measured at **max channel delta 4 of 255, mean 1.01, zero pixels differing by more than 32**: rounding between two rasterizers, not a defect. *Recognition cue:* **a defect that is perfectly reproducible inside one worktree and has no independent witness outside it.** The build tree is the suspect precisely because every arm shares it, and a positive control built in the same tree inherits the fault (the same shape as trap 122, one level lower). *Fix used:* re-measure from a worktree configured from scratch before believing any renderer finding. **Corollary learned the hard way: the lane's build directory was destroyed in routine worktree cleanup before this was suspected, so the mechanism here is inferred rather than demonstrated — when a build tree's OUTPUT is the finding, the tree is evidence; keep it until the finding is independently reproduced.** #3374 |
| 268 | **A per-event value that the API populates even when the event never produced it — RenderDoc's `shaderOut` on a fragment that was rejected BEFORE the shader ran carries the PREVIOUS event's colour** | Measured on a purpose-built control (`tools/pixel_history/control.c`) whose draw 3 is white and scissored away from the probe pixel: RenderDoc reported its `shaderOut` as `[0,0,1,1]` — the blue of draw 2. Every field was well-formed and plausible; nothing was null, zero or flagged. *Why it is dangerous:* the reader is asking "what did the shader compute here", and a non-black answer on a black frame reads as `STORE_LOST_IT` — blend state, write masks — sending them to entirely the wrong file. On a game capture it is undetectable, because the true colour of a scissored draw is exactly what you do not know. *Recognition cue:* a rejected event whose rejection reason is positional (`scissorClipped`, `backfaceCulled`, `depthClipped`, `viewClipped`, `sampleMasked`, `predicationSkipped`) has no shader output by definition — no fragment ran. *Fix:* `pixel_history.py` suppresses shader output for those reasons and prints `suppressed (no fragment ran)`. The general form is the reason the control exists at all: **a tool that reads a value cannot tell you the value is meaningful; only a construction whose answer you already know can.** #3402 |
| 269 | **A `vkCmdClearColorImage` is reported by RenderDoc's pixel history as a PASSING modification, so a clear inside the captured frame gives every pixel a passing event** | Measured while building `tools/pixel_history/control.c`: a region whose every draw was shader-discarded read `SHADER_WROTE_BLACK` rather than `ALL_REJECTED`, because the transfer clear (eid 4) sat in its history as a pass with a black postMod. The comment in the control had asserted the opposite — that a transfer clear "is not itself a pixel-history event" — which was reasoning, not measurement. *Why it matters beyond the control:* any real frame that clears its target hands every pixel a passing black event, so "the last passing event computed black" can be the CLEAR rather than a shader, and a verdict keyed on it would blame shading for a pixel nothing ever drew to. *Fix:* the control now submits its ground clear before `StartFrameCapture`, so the captured frame is draws-only; readers of real captures should expect the clear to appear and not mistake it for a draw. #3402 |

**Detecting the generated-prose trap (#27), because it generalises past `.pad` files:**

When several artifacts claim *independent* findings, **diff their substance, not their prose**. Identical
bodies under differing explanations means the explanations were written to sound like observations:

```bash
# strip the commentary and compare only the load-bearing content
for d in joe-mac asterix summer-sports; do grep -v '^#' "$d/reach-gameplay.pad" | md5sum; done
```

Seconds of work invalidated three documents. The same check applies to route scripts, per-title status
docs, issue comments and PR bodies — anywhere parallel artifacts assert parallel results.

**It also happens in source comments, which is where it does the most damage.** "It happens in `.pad`
files" understates the trap; a second lane hit it the same day in `hle_service.cpp`. Recording the
first live confirmation of `VdecConfig` (#1687), the comment claimed the guest's values were
"individually meaningful at **every** offset" and that a wrong field order "**could not**" produce
them. Checking the pairs one at a time instead of asserting the general claim: `profile=100` /
`max_level=41` cannot swap (41 is not a `profile_idc`, 100 is not a `level_idc`), `max_dpb` /
`input_depth` cannot (depth would go negative), width/height cannot (the movie would be portrait) —
and `resource` / `codec` are **both `1`** in that title, so that one pair is not discriminated at all
and still rests on the commit that introduced it. The sweeping version was written first and read as
established fact.

A doc carries a `docs/` path and some ambient suspicion; a source comment sitting above a
`static_assert` reads as verified by construction, nothing marks it as inference, and every future
reader inherits it. So when a comment records evidence, **enumerate what the evidence forces and name
what it does not** — "HIGH except this pair, which is MED, and a non-AVC title settles it in one line
of log" is a comment the next reader can act on. A general confidence claim is one they cannot check.

This is **worse than a stale `UNVALIDATED` banner**, and for a specific reason: a banner tells the reader
to check. A confident, specific, plausible header tells the reader **not to bother**, so it survives every
subsequent reading. The remedy is not to flip a banner but to **add accurate provenance where a confident
claim has none** — hold a route header to the `scripts/greak/reach-gameplay.pad` standard, which cites
*observed* phase timings ("the title screen appears at roughly 112 s on a Linux hardware-Vulkan boot")
rather than asserted behaviour. A claim marked ✅ on a run nobody can reproduce is the same defect one
level up.

**Working rules that follow:**

- **A decoded-draw census is meaningless without knowing the render phase, and needs a positive control.**
  Menu, loading, and 2D-UI phases legitimately decode single-digit to low-double-digit draws per frame, so a
  low count is evidence of nothing on its own. Count **per sample interval**, not per run — a whole-run
  average describes neither regime (Blue Prince's is 622.7, which is neither its 10 nor its 2,200) — and
  calibrate against a title **known to render the thing you claim is missing**. Best of all is one control
  exhibiting *both* regimes in a single run, which rules out per-title decode differences as the
  explanation for the gap. Then confirm the phase by **opening the frames**: #1641's Nikoderiko row was
  only interpretable once the images showed samples 10-11 carrying ~160k distinct colours while 3-9 were
  black, which proved the census was tracking real workload rather than drifting.
- **Build a timing measurement that cannot lie quietly.** Everything above is "this instrument lied"; this is
  the counterpart — the shape of a measurement that reports its own invalidity instead of averaging it away.
  Seven lanes share one GPU, so vigilance does not scale: a run *will* eventually overlap someone else's
  capture, and a contended sample looks exactly like a real regression. Three properties, all cheap:
  1. **Self-lock** (`flock` on a lockfile) so a second copy of the driver refuses to start rather than racing
     the first. Two waiters queued behind the same "GPU free" condition both fire when it clears.
  2. **Build strictly before measuring, never concurrently.** A `-j8` compile landing on top of a run costs
     more throughput than whatever is being measured. Put the build inside the driver, ahead of the loop.
  3. **Discard, don't average.** Sample the foreign-process count **before and after** each run; if either is
     nonzero, **rename the log to `DISCARD-…`** instead of dropping it. Contention then becomes *visible in
     the record* rather than invisible in the mean, and the discarded run stays auditable afterwards instead
     of surviving as an unexplained outlier.
  4. **Alternate the arms, then check the residuals for cycle correlation** before attributing any
     difference to the treatment. Running all of arm A then all of arm B lets a *time-varying* confound
     land entirely on one arm and become a phantom effect. Alternating forces it onto both, which makes it
     detectable: in #1609's 12-run experiment the slowest run was **cycle 2 in all four title × arm
     combinations**, so the residual variance tracked *when* a run happened, not *which arm* it was. That is
     not an argument that the gap is noise — it is a demonstration, from the same data that would otherwise
     have produced the phantom. If the extremes line up by cycle rather than by arm, the treatment is not
     the explanation.
  Report **whole-run and steady-state (tail-window) rates separately**: a one-off boot cost and a per-frame
  cost are indistinguishable in a whole-run average, because a run that reaches steady state one second later
  and then performs identically still shows a lower average. Report min/max spread and an explicit
  **overlap verdict** across arms — if the arms overlap, say so plainly and the question is closed.
- **Joining two instruments requires an explicit shared ordinal, never stream adjacency.** If two diagnostics
  are emitted at different pipeline stages, their interleaving in the log is an artifact of *when each stage
  ran*, not of when the events happened. Print a common ordinal (`command_order`) on both and join on it. This
  is why the `order=` field exists on the `[exec] skip draw early` line — **it is not redundant with the
  register values on the same line, and removing it silently re-opens the phantom above.**
- **A guard keyed on a threshold has an expiry date and gives no signal at expiry.** Key it on the thing
  itself. #1675 added a check that CI must register at least 160 tests, so a configure that quietly lost
  Vulkan would fail instead of reporting a smaller green suite. It read as the careful part of the change
  and it was decoration with a delay: CI has 138 non-Vulkan tests, the suite only grows, and once ~22 more
  exist, losing all 27 Vulkan tests still clears 160. The guard would have stopped protecting the thing it
  was added for, at some unremarkable future commit, silently — the same shape as the defect it was
  guarding against. Replaced with a check that three **named** Vulkan-gated tests are registered: they
  cannot exist unless Vulkan was found, and that stays true at any suite size. The general form — **a
  threshold derived from today's numbers decays as the system grows; assert the property, not a count that
  happens to imply it today** — and the corollary: verify a guard fires in **both** directions, because
  one that cannot fail is worse than none.
- **Before recording that something cannot be measured, check the tools you are claiming cannot measure
  it.** This is nastier than a wrong fact and it is self-sealing. A wrong claim about the *subject* gets
  tested by the next measurement; a wrong claim about the *instrument* stops the measurement from
  happening, so nothing ever contradicts it. #1635 shipped "severity is unknowable — `self_dump` has no
  import listing" into a PR body. `self_dump` prints `[IMPORTS BY LIBRARY]`, documented in
  `tools/re/README.md`, and answering the question took ten minutes once someone looked: 21 aliased NIDs,
  **0** imported by anything. An "unanswerable" is a claim about a tool, and claims about tools are the
  cheapest of all to verify — run `--help`, grep the README, read the source. The same session produced
  the mirror image in #1675: CI's green tick was believed to mean "the suite passed" when it meant "the
  138 tests that got registered passed", and the 32 missing ones — ~1,822 assertions — were invisible
  because nothing reports a test that was never registered.
- **A fix that makes an instrument lie more convincingly is worse than the bug.** This one is about
  *repairs*, not measurements, and it is the inverse of everything else on this page. #1659's own fix
  briefly introduced it: the diagnostics were widened to resolve any guest module, but several kept a
  hard-coded `eboot+` label. Before the change an Il2Cpp frame printed an offset *larger than the image* —
  the exact tell the entry below tells you to look for. After it, the same frame printed a small,
  plausible, in-range offset under the **wrong module name**, with no tell at all. The fault was
  unchanged; only the symptom was removed. Any correction that deletes a symptom without deleting the
  fault produces a **quieter** failure, and quiet failures are the expensive ones. When you fix an
  instrument, ask what its wrongness used to look like and confirm you removed the wrongness rather than
  the appearance of it. Corollary for tests: a test that exercises the *helper* you added will not catch
  this — it has to render through the real formatter and assert the text a reader would see.
- **A `<module>+0x<rva>` offset larger than the module's image is a labelling artifact, not a wild pointer.**
  Guest module bases are fixed constants in `src/host/image/boot_program.hpp`, and they *move*: #825 relocated the
  eboot from `0x400000000` to `0x410000000` so Astro Bot's direct-memory mapping would stop aliasing code.
  Diagnostics that had hard-coded the old literal kept printing `eboot+0x…` against it, so every offset was
  the real RVA **plus `0x10000000`** — an offset past the end of a 161 MB image, and one that no longer
  round-tripped through `PROSPER_BP` (which adds the *mapped* base). #1659 converged every printer onto
  `prosper::guest_module_name()` / `guest_module_offset()`; if you are hand-computing a base anywhere, that
  is the bug. Two corollaries worth keeping: an address below the lowest module base is **data, not code**
  (that region is now a DMEM aperture), and a single wide range test labelled every module in it "eboot",
  so a wrong *binary* is as likely as a wrong offset. Static tools are unaffected — `xref.py`, `edis.py` and
  `prx_to_elf.py` compute from ELF `p_vaddr`/file offsets and never add a runtime base, so RVAs recorded
  from disassembly stay trustworthy.
- **Before quoting a diagnostic's line count as a rate, grep for its cap.** These sites use a
  `static std::atomic<int>` counter or a per-key dedupe set; check for one before concluding "this fires N
  times" or "this is rare". When the site prints its own counter — `WaitRegMem #%d` *is* `ln` — the true
  total is free: the highest `#N` in the log bounds it, and a run whose maximum stays under the cap has
  printed *every* occurrence. That read turned #1606's "dozens per second" into 18 and 21 events for two
  entire runs.

- **Before concluding that geometry is missing, establish what content is actually resident.** A draw
  count describes the frame; it says nothing about whether the world has anything in it. For a
  UE4/pak title this is one offline command against a log you already have —
  `tools/re/pak_index.py GAME.pak --log run.log --distinct` resolves the `[apr] read-submit` byte
  offsets to asset names, so "which maps/blueprints/widgets did the guest load, and where did
  loading stop?" is answered with no boot and no GPU. Run it *first*. On #1641 it dissolved a
  session's worth of GPU investigation in one pass.
- **Read an unimplemented call's *arguments* before inferring what it is doing.** This is cheap and
  almost never done. `PROSPER_STUBDUMP=1` prints one line per import — `[stub] #877 off=0x148e0
  libScePosix::T8fER+tIGgk` — and each stub lives at `0x600000000 + off`, so a gdb breakpoint at the
  **stub entry** catches the guest's argument registers fully intact (the stub has not clobbered
  anything yet; note the generic unimplemented path *does* overwrite `rdi`/`rsi`/`rdx` by the time it
  reaches `prosper_on_unimpl`, so break on the stub, not the C++ handler):

  ```bash
  PROSPER_STUBDUMP=1 … boot_trace <dump> 2>&1 | grep '<NID>'      # -> off=0x148e0
  gdb -p $(pgrep -x boot_trace) -batch -ex 'set pagination off' \
      -ex 'break *0x6000148e0' -ex continue -ex 'info registers rdi rsi rdx rcx r8'
  ```

  The stub table is per-run — re-read it, never reuse an address. On PPSA19244 this converted
  "1.3 billion socket polls" into "a 3-second sleep that returns instantly", and stopped a
  substantial piece of shared work from being scoped against a wrong premise.
- **A trap you have already recorded is the one most likely to catch you.** Trap 21 is trap 14, hit
  by the lane that wrote trap 14 down, because the rule had been internalised as "check the render
  *phase*" when the general form is "check the *denominator* — phase, resident content, and engine
  architecture — before treating a low count as a defect."
- Prefer experiments that **detect their own invalidity** — e.g. render two adjacent operations and assert both
  return the same resolution, so a surface switch invalidates the comparison instead of producing a phantom.
- **Open the image.** Believe neither a metric win nor a metric failure without looking. A diagnostic clear can
  out-score real content on colour count; a healthy frame can fail SSIM on animation phase.
- A measurement that is **right for one question can be silently wrong for the next one asked of the same data**.
- Never re-derive a bisect boundary without confirming both sides render the **same target**.
- Check whether a later comment or merged PR already **superseded** an inherited premise before acting on it
  (Bendy's "CPU software-detile dominates" had been overtaken by #1179/#1190/#1269 in its own issue).
- Write `Refs #NN`, not `Fixes #NN`, when a PR only partially addresses an issue — a parenthetical qualifier does
  **not** stop GitHub's auto-close, and #1554 was closed that way despite a comment saying to keep it open.

### State only what is true at the moment and scope you state it

**A statement should only assert what is true at the moment and scope it is made. A line that announces an
*intention* in the grammar of a *fact* is indistinguishable from a result — and both humans and agents act on it
as one.** A confident statement reads identically whether or not it is earned, so nothing announces itself when
it stops being true.

Instances, all the same shape:

- The F9 arming line announced a filename it could not guarantee. A grab that aborted then left a `.bmp` beside a
  same-named `.prgbundle` from a different boot 51 minutes earlier, and a lane was handed the two as a matched
  pair. Fixed by naming the title at arm time and the real path per artifact after each file exists (#1693).
- A PR body asserted a `CONFIDENCE: HIGH` the source had already retracted; merging would have written the
  withdrawn version into the permanent record.
- An issue title asserted a mechanism that had been falsified, and kept sending agents at it for a day.

The practical form:

- If you cannot guarantee it when you write it, **state what you do know** and emit the fact when you have it.
- If a claim rests on three examples, **say "verified on three cases"** — not "for every input".
- A document, title, or comment that outlives its reasoning becomes **an assertion nobody is checking**.
  Retitling a falsified issue and correcting a stale PR body are correctness work, not tidying.

Same family as trap #34 (a check of the *answer* read as a check of the *rule*) and the source-comment case
under the generated-prose trap above: those are claims whose **scope** outran their evidence, this is claims
whose **moment** outran theirs. In both the grammar is what does the damage — nothing in a confident sentence
distinguishes the earned version from the unearned one.

### A test seam that pins a policy makes its env-var A/B silently void

An A/B was run by setting `PROSPER_ULT_RETURN_SUCCESS=1` to force libSceUlt's mutex to a no-op, expecting the
mutual-exclusion test to fail. **It passed**, which read as "the test is too weak to detect a broken lock" — a
believable and entirely wrong conclusion. The test calls `ult_set_return_success_for_test(false)` in its own
`main()`, deliberately, so that the environment cannot decide whether it passes. That seam overrode the variable,
so the "no-op" arm was never a no-op; both arms ran the same real mutex. Re-run with the pin removed, the
original test caught the broken lock immediately (27,984 of 40,000 increments surviving).

**The rule: any A/B driven by an environment variable is void until you prove the variable actually took effect.**
Have the run print the policy it is operating under, or assert the arm's expected *behaviour* before trusting its
result. This is the same shape as trap #10 (a switch that is right for one question and silently wrong for the
next) and trap #7 (a stale label read as state): a control that legitimately exists for one purpose silently
neutralises a measurement made for another.

Two corollaries worth keeping:

- The tell is an A/B where **the arms agree when they must differ**. Treat that as an instrument failure first,
  not a finding. A negative control that cannot fail is not a control.
- A test that pins its own configuration is still the right design — it stops the environment deciding the
  verdict. The defect is using that same variable as the A/B lever. Give the A/B its own lever, or remove the pin
  for the experiment and say so.

### A host function is not a valid stand-in for guest code — and only Windows will tell you

A test for libSceUlt's ulthreads used an ordinary host C++ function as the ulthread entry. It passed on
Linux and macOS and failed on **Windows MinGW only**, for three hours, on the single assertion that read the
entry's argument back out.

The guest is **System V AMD64**; prosper enters guest code through `prosper_call_guest_on_stack`, which
marshals into SysV (first argument in `%rdi`). On Linux and macOS the host ABI *is* System V, so a host
function is an accurate model and the test is sound by accident. On Windows the host is **Microsoft x64**,
where the first argument arrives in `%rcx` — so the untagged host function read a garbage argument and
returned a garbage status **while still executing correctly and touching its stack**, which is what made it
look like it worked. The product was never wrong; the test was. `win_thread_trampoline` already documents
this exact hazard for the production path ("a bare `pthread_create(entry, arg)` mis-passes the arg (MS x64
vs SysV)").

**The rule: any test that supplies a function for prosper to call *as guest code* must tag it with the guest
convention**, `__attribute__((sysv_abi))` on x86-64, not leave it at the host default. Prefer the tag over an
`#ifdef` around the body: "this function uses the guest's calling convention" is true on every platform and
the attribute is simply redundant where the native ABI already matches. Keep such entries plain leaf
functions — no destructors, no exceptions — because the attribute conflicts with MinGW's SEH-based C++
unwinding, which is why `PROSPER_SYSV_ABI` is empty for the HLE handlers (see `dispatch.hpp`).

The generalisable half: **a green Linux run says nothing about ABI-boundary code.** Guest entries, callbacks
the guest invokes, and anything reached through the call-guest shims are the cases where the dev platform
cannot fail and Windows is the only place the bug is visible.

### Registering a NID with an error return can be worse than leaving it unregistered

A trap of the same family as the list above, but in the HLE surface rather than an instrument. It cost a
merged defect (#1618, introduced by #1614), so it belongs beside them.

The dispatcher's default for an **unresolved** NID is `return 0` (`hle/dispatch.cpp` `prosper_on_unimpl`).
That default is safe for a function whose contract returns a **value**, and unsafe for one that returns a
**status** — which is exactly why fail-visible registration is usually an improvement. The trap is that the
inverse is equally true, and easy to miss: **an error sentinel is unsafe for a value-returning contract,
because such a signature has no error channel and the sentinel is read as data.**

`sceUltWaitingQueueResourcePoolGetWorkAreaSize` returns a `size_t` in `rax` which the guest passes straight
to `malloc`. Registering it to return `SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_ENOSYS` therefore asked for a 2.0 GiB allocation
where the unregistered default had produced a harmless `malloc(0)` — the #544/#660 class, reintroduced by
the very change meant to prevent it.

**The rule:** before registering a NID purely to make it visible, establish from the call site whether its
contract returns a status or a value. If it returns a value and the real one is unknown, the dispatcher's
`0` — or better, an honest computed value — is correct, and the visibility belongs in the log line, not the
return. Disassembling one call site is enough: `call …; mov [rbp-N],rax; …; call malloc` settles it in
seconds, and no amount of reasoning about the function's *name* substitutes for it.

### Proving "the guest never touches X" — pick the exhaustive instrument, not the plausible one

Recurring question when deciding whether prosper may write bookkeeping into a guest-owned struct: *does guest
code ever read inside it?* Answering it for libSceUlt (#1603) produced one sound instrument and one tempting
unsound one; the difference is worth reusing.

**Unsound:** identify the functions that operate on the owning C++ class by the displacements they use
(`[reg+0x398]`, `[reg+0x410]`, …), then look for accesses inside the member's range. Displacement sets are not
identities — `memcpy`'s bulk AVX stores and every function's own `[rsp+0x...]` stack frame match them. This
reported **1,361** then **707** hits, all false, and tightening the heuristic eventually matched *nothing*,
which is equally uninformative. A heuristic that can be tuned from "everything" to "nothing" is measuring the
tuning, not the subject.

**Sound:** enumerate the objects' actual addresses, then scan **every** RIP-relative reference in the whole
executable segment and ask which land at a non-zero offset from one. That is exhaustive over the only
addressing mode that can reach a static object, so zero hits is a proof rather than an absence of evidence. It
found 16 static Ult objects and **zero** interior references — every reference a `lea` of the base feeding an
Ult call.

Generalising: prefer the instrument whose **negative result is exhaustive over a closed set** (all
RIP-relative references) to one that pattern-matches an **open set** (all functions that might alias a
pointer). When only the open-set instrument exists, its negative result is a hypothesis, not a finding.

### Cross-title: the colour-state registers are decoded correctly — start elsewhere

Two lanes independently established this, and both results killed a live hypothesis in a *different*
lane. Recorded here rather than in either lane because the next agent to see a zero colour write mask
will reach for the same explanation.

**`MODE` decode is not remapped.** A tempting hypothesis on Astro Bot (#1459) was that prosper mis-maps
the `CB_COLOR_CONTROL` `MODE` enum — its live trace never once observed `MODE=1 (CB_NORMAL)`, only modes
0 and 6, which is not what an ordinary renderer looks like. **Blue Prince falsifies it**: that title
writes `MODE=1 CB_NORMAL` and renders correctly through the same decode. Any remapping hypothesis now
has to explain Blue Prince too, which no simple remap does.

**`CB_COLOR_CONTROL` tracking is not lossy.** An Oregon Trail investigation measured the indirect
register path directly: **~12,000 observed `CB_COLOR_CONTROL` writes**, `MODE=1` dominant at roughly
**7:1**, and — joined on **each draw's own `command_order`** — **zero mismatches across all 1,050
suppressed draws**. The register prosper believes is in force at a draw is the register the guest wrote.

**So "prosper is misreading MODE" starts from a losing position.** When a draw resolves `cwm=0`, the
productive question is **who wrote the zero and when**, not whether the decode is right.

**The join is the trap in that second result, and it is worth its own line.** The same measurement read
the *other* way — joining `PROSPER_REGWATCH` against `PROSPER_EXECLOG` by **stream adjacency** rather
than by `command_order` — reported that 1,049 of 1,050 suppressed draws resolved against a **future**
register write. Clean, compelling, and entirely false: the two instruments timestamp at different
pipeline stages (REGWATCH at command-processor *decode*, EXECLOG at *realization*) and the phases
interleave per submit. See instrument-trap 12; #1633's `order=` is what makes the honest join possible.

### Cross-title: aggregate frame metrics cannot tell an animating menu from gameplay

A bring-up run that reports healthy frame statistics has not thereby reported that it reached
anything. On *Asterix & Obelix: Babylon Mission* (2026-08-19) a run that reached the `World_3_10`
harbour and a run that sat on the `SoloCoopMenu` character-select screen for 495 s were **identical**
on every aggregate the capture tool produces — same route file, same binary, same dump:

```
skip2    60/60  source-distinct=60 pixel-distinct=58 stale=0.0s guest=running status=ok  -> gameplay
repro3   60/60  source-distinct=60 pixel-distinct=58 stale=0.0s guest=running status=ok  -> character select
```

**An animating menu is exactly as pixel-distinct as a level**, and `status=ok` reports the harness's
health rather than the guest's progress. Frame count, pixel-distinctness, staleness and liveness are
each necessary, and not one of them is a discriminator. A route validated on those alone is validated
against nothing.

**So state a content-level success condition BEFORE the run** — a scene token, a level or map name, a
HUD element that exists only in gameplay — and read the aggregates as a check that the run was *valid*,
never as evidence of what it *reached*.

**For Unity titles there is a cheap and self-checking one.** `Media/levelNN` is the Unity
build-settings scene index: on that title, 76 scenes against 76 files, confirmed by a byte-level scan,
and self-checking because each world level appears as a `World_X_Y` + `World_X_Y_Level` pair — so a
mis-derived index surfaces as a broken pairing rather than as a plausible wrong answer. Twelve of the
fourteen titles at gameplay are Unity/IL2CPP, so it generalises. The scene sequence was the **only**
instrument that separated the two runs above; the successful one went Loading -> region map -> level,
the others Loading -> `SoloCoopMenu` -> stuck. Full derivation in
[`ASTERIX_BABYLON_STATUS.md`](ASTERIX_BABYLON_STATUS.md); the route-reliability defect it exposed is #2743.

**A cheaper self-check when the scene names do not pair: the `.resS` partition.** Asterix's check is
the `World_X_Y` + `World_X_Y_Level` pairing, which only exists because that title names its scenes
that way. *Beneath* (`PPSA27640`) does not, and a second check was still available for free from the
dump's own file list: a `levelNN.resS` sidecar holds a scene's streamed resource data, so it attaches
to heavy world scenes and not to loading screens. On that title 46 scene paths match 46 `levelNN`
files, and **every one of the 12 `.resS` files above `level5` lands on a world scene while not one
lands on a loading screen** — in a dump that is more than half loading screens. A mis-derived index
breaks that partition rather than yielding a plausible wrong answer, which is the property that makes
a check worth having. `sharedassetsN` gives a third reading on titles that pair it per scene (see
[`BENDY_DARK_REVIVAL_STATUS.md`](BENDY_DARK_REVIVAL_STATUS.md)). Full derivation:
[`BENEATH_STATUS.md`](BENEATH_STATUS.md).

**Establish that the oracle discriminates before relying on it — the failures are not exotic.** It
has now been used on four titles and been void on two: *Tales of Graces f* ships exactly one level
file, so the index is a constant, and *Bendy and the Ink Machine*'s layout does not transfer to its
own sequel. Counting the title's own `levelNN` files is a one-command precondition, not a formality.

**For Unreal titles the equivalent is the IoStore package stream, and it is now tooled.** A large
share of the titles still short of gameplay are Unreal — read `COMPATIBILITY.md` for the current
split rather than a number restated here — and the Unity oracle covers none of them. The Unreal
analogue is *which package the guest read*, decoded from a `PROSPER_FILELOG=1` stream by
`tools/re/iostore_index.py` (`--maps` for just the `.umap` sequence). It is self-checking in the same
way: Unreal cooked package paths are semantic, so on `PPSA17942` the title screen reads
`Map/Product/Title/Title_PL.umap` — which `DefaultEngine.ini`'s own `GameDefaultMap` names as the
startup map — and a town reads a cluster of
`Map/Product/World/Field/F001/C001/Modern/C001_M_Env_*.umap` sub-levels. (Note the era folder:
the path without `Modern/` matches **nothing** in the container, and a glob that quietly returns
zero is the easiest way to mistake a working oracle for a broken one.) A mis-derived mapping produces nonsense rather than a plausible wrong answer.

Two things make this different from just pointing `pak_index.py` at the container. First, an
IoStore title's `.pak` holds **no packages at all** — only configs, fonts and localization — so
`pak_index.py` correctly reports zero maps and names nothing; the packages are in the `.ucas`.
Second, a chunk's offset in the `.utoc` is in the container's **logical** space while a read syscall
carries a **physical** offset into the packed `.ucas`, and on `PPSA17942` the two differ by 4.1 GB at
the startup map alone — so a one-step lookup misnames most of a stream while still looking plausible.
`iostore_index.py --self-test` pins that mapping and is registered in ctest.

**And test the phase, not the membership — a front-loading title reads its first world's packages
before you ever leave the menus.** Measured on `PPSA17942` (2026-08-20): the town sub-levels
`T001_M_Env_GOut`, `T001_M_Gmk_GOut` and `T001_M_PartyTalk_GIns` are all read at **58 s**, while the
run is still on the save-slot screen and roughly ten minutes before the world is entered; two
further World maps are read at 0.0 s, *before* the title map. A "did it read a World map" predicate
would have reported gameplay on a run that never left a menu — the exact failure the oracle exists
to prevent, arrived at through the oracle. What separates the states is **bytes per minute by
content directory**: 0.25 MB of Environment while the setup menus are up against 285 MB in the
minute the world loads, and a persistent level (`T001_M_PL.umap`) plus a level-sequence package
appearing only in that window. On a title that streams on demand the membership form is enough;
check which kind you have before quoting it.

Validate it the way the `## Ruled out` rule requires: build a positive instance **by hand from the
container index**, outside any run — compute a known package's physical offset and check that
`--resolve` names it back — rather than trusting that the instrument fires because a run produced
some output.

## The orchestration contract

### Orchestrator responsibilities

The orchestrator owns integration and scheduling, not every line of investigation. It should:

1. Read `CLAUDE.md`, this document, the title status document, and the active issue before assigning work.
2. Fetch `origin/main` and give every subagent a private worktree and a fresh branch from the exact remote head.
3. Assign one title or one precisely bounded shared-infrastructure question per subagent.
4. Prevent duplicated hypotheses by requiring agents to read the current issue evidence before running anything.
5. Keep each agent on an evidence ladder: retained offline artifact first, bounded live capture only when the artifact
   cannot answer the question, implementation only after a generic behavioral contract is identified.
6. Decide GPU scheduling. Ordinary correctness replays may overlap; reserve exclusivity only when measurements,
   memory pressure, or observed interference require it.
7. Review every proposed diff itself, including assumptions, scope, tests, `git diff --check`, and exact base/head.
8. Open short-lived PRs for proven progress, wait for every applicable CI job, confirm release publication skipped on
   an ordinary PR, then merge when authorized.
9. Keep issues and PR descriptions self-contained. Post exact commands, hashes, conclusions, and falsified hypotheses.
10. Update compatibility docs and representative screenshots when a title reaches a new visible checkpoint.
11. Rebase or restart long-lived investigation branches from current `main` before they drift.
12. Stop stale processes and keep evidence off the repository and off RAM-backed `/tmp`.

The current user explicitly authorized the orchestrator/subagent arrangement as pair programming and allowed the
independent-review step to be skipped for PRs produced by this coordinated work. The orchestrator still has to inspect
the complete diff, verify the exact head, wait for all CI, and merge deliberately. Treat this as engagement-specific
authorization: if a future user has not granted it, follow the independent-review default in `CLAUDE.md`.

### Subagent responsibilities

Every game subagent should receive and follow this contract:

- Work only in the assigned private worktree and worktree-local build directory.
- Start from the exact remote `main` SHA supplied by the orchestrator; report branch, head, merge-base, and status.
- Read the title issue and status docs before running a new experiment.
- State one falsifiable question before each run and the outcome that would distinguish the competing explanations.
- Prefer immutable F9/timeline/capsule evidence and offline `gpu_replay` over repeated game boots.
